# Limitations

Consolidated from docs/log by research/scripts/consolidate_log.py. Edit the entries, not this file.

## Failures

- **2026-10-17, S0.1, failure.** Running `biome migrate --write` on Biome 2.5.15 to replace the deprecated `rules.recommended: true` produced `rules.preset: "none"`, which turns every lint rule off. Consequence: Any future Biome migration is followed by reading `biome.json` back and running a probe before merging.
- **2026-10-17, S0.1, failure.** The first CI run of S0.1 failed before any step ran because `astral-sh/setup-uv@v10` does not resolve. Consequence: Before adding an action, check that the tag exists (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/matching-refs/tags/v`).
- **2026-10-17, S0.3, failure.** On Windows, the check scripts crashed with a libuv assertion (`!(handle->flags & UV_HANDLE_CLOSING)`, `src\win\async.c`) when they called `process.exit(1)` straight after writing problems to stderr. Consequence: Scripts in this repository set `process.exitCode` and never call `process.exit` after writing output.
- **2026-10-17, S0.5, failure.** The S0.5 session fixture recorded on Windows failed on Linux CI. Consequence: Session fixtures are accepted only after a green run on both Windows and Linux.
- **2026-10-22, S0.10, failure.** The test proving the pi-import lint rule timed out in the full suite although it passed alone. Consequence: Tests that spawn tools set an explicit timeout.
- **2026-10-22, S0.11, failure.** The S0.11 commit was made and pushed while `make lint` failed on a TypeScript error in the conformance suite. Consequence: Commits are chained on `make lint && make test`, so a failing gate stops the commit; CI would also have caught it.
- **2026-10-22, S0.12, failure.** The tag `prereg-v0` was first created and pushed on commit 7515b0d, the S0.11 typing fix, instead of the pre-registration commit. Consequence: A tag is created only in the same chain as the commit it marks, after the commit succeeds.
- **2026-10-25, A1, failure.** After `pnpm add mem0ai@3.3.1`, Mem0's local vector store could not open: pnpm 10 ignored the install script of `better-sqlite3`, so its native binding was never built. Consequence: A machine with no prebuilt binary for its platform needs a C++ toolchain for node-gyp.
- **2026-10-25, A1, failure.** The Mem0 adapter switched Mem0's telemetry off only when it first loaded `mem0ai/oss` itself, so a program that loaded Mem0 earlier kept telemetry on (review finding R4-7). Consequence: AGENTS.md rule 12 holds for the Mem0 adapter whatever the import order; a Mem0 upgrade that renames these methods fails loudly.
- **2026-10-25, A1, failure.** The first test of mem0.test.ts, the write, search and delete cycle, timed out at vitest's 5 s default in one of three full parallel suite runs, and passed when run alone. Consequence: The import moves out of the test body; see 2026-10-25-a1-mem0-test-warm-load.md.
- **2026-10-25, A4, failure, H4.** The conformance suite as first written for card A4 passed twelve adapters with planted bugs, among them dropping grants, nulling the validity window, truncating content to 200 characters, listing by scope prefix and accepting another adapter's ref on delete. Consequence: The meta-test stays committed, so a weakened case or corpus fails the build. Store results on H4 rest on a suite that checks every field.
- **2026-10-25, B1, failure.** The first run of `packages/bench/scripts/run-gatemem.ts` failed with `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_TYPESCRIPT_SYNTAX` on the reference agent's constructor. Consequence: Code reached by `node <file>.ts` avoids parameter properties, enums and namespaces.
- **2026-10-25, B1, failure.** The first strict GateMem checkpoint schema, written from the medical domain's fields, rejected office checkpoint line 106 when the full upstream dataset was loaded. Consequence: Field inventories are taken over every domain's full file before a schema is called strict.
- **2026-10-25, E0.3, failure.** The first version of `NotImplementedYet` used TypeScript constructor parameter properties, and `make lint` failed in tsc with TS1294. Consequence: None beyond the fix; core code avoids enums, namespaces and parameter properties.
- **2026-10-25, E2.1, failure.** The first syntax-error test for card E2.1, an unterminated double quote on line 11 of a policy, failed: the error was reported at line 31. Consequence: Policy authors may see an end-of-file line for an unclosed quote; the message names the missing quote, which is enough to find it.
- **2026-10-25, E2.2, failure, H1.** With the real Plane in place, invariant 1 as written in step-0 task S0.9 passed while testing almost nothing: in its 300 seeded cases no read returned a single item. Consequence: Any future property over the read path should count what it exercised, not only what it rejected.
- **2026-10-25, E8.2, failure.** The first `engram read` showed no fixture memory, and the first fix listed the folder through the adapter from the CLI and wrapped the adapter to return existing store_refs. Consequence: Surfaces bring existing store content under the plane only through core.
- **2026-10-25, E8.2, failure.** Two things failed when the CLI first ran as a process: Node printed an ExperimentalWarning for node:sqlite on stderr, and `pnpm --filter cli exec engram` answered that the command was not found. Consequence: AGENTS.md lists `make engram` as the way to run the CLI from the repository.
- **2026-10-25, E2.2, failure, H1.** The first strengthened invariant 1 and the first read-leak property passed while still missing the cases they were written for. Consequence: A guard should count the exact condition a mutant needs, not a proxy for it.
- **2026-10-25, B1, failure.** The first version of `py.ts` declared `PyInt` and `PyFloat` with TypeScript parameter properties, and `make bench` crashed with ERR_UNSUPPORTED_TYPESCRIPT_SYNTAX. Consequence: A lint rule against parameter properties outside test files would catch this before a spawned test does; not added in this pass.
- **2026-10-25, PX1, failure.** Undoing a mutation check with `git checkout` on packages/core/src/plane/write.ts also discarded the uncommitted checkWrite refactor in that file. Consequence: Mutants are undone from a copy, never with checkout, while work is uncommitted.
- **2026-10-25, PX1, failure.** The first PX1 commit (951850c, rebased as 9ec5760) fails its own tests. Consequence: A change that alters a golden file regenerates it in the same commit, or the commit message names the commit that does.
- **2026-10-25, PX1, failure.** While mutation-checking the PX1 acceptance test, the restore step used `git checkout` on an uncommitted file, which put back the S0.5 extension and discarded the new one; a copy taken before the mutation restored it. The first `make lint` also failed on two type casts. Consequence: Mutation checks restore from a copy, never from git, while work is uncommitted.
- **2026-10-25, RN1, failure.** On its first run, the RN1 probe read the episode's turns from B1's agent view of the episode. That view has no turns, so 4 of the 5 acceptance cases failed with a TypeError. Consequence: Only the harness reads the full episode. What the session shows the model is still the stripped view.
- **2026-10-25, S0.3, failure.** Both cases of scripts/test/fixtures-ci.test.ts timed out at vitest's 5 s default in full parallel `make test` runs, without a code change, and passed when run alone. Consequence: The cases get an explicit timeout; see 2026-10-25-s0-3-fixtures-ci-explicit-timeout.md.
- **2026-10-28, A4, failure.** Two helper scripts failed on Windows text encoding during round 2: a corpus edit piped through a Bash heredoc, and the mutation runner's progress output. Consequence: Helper scripts on this machine are written to files and set UTF-8 output themselves.
- **2026-10-28, E2.3, failure.** The first version of the test that a failed store write does not stall the write queue (finding N2) was wrong. It passed before the queue existed and failed once the queue was in. Consequence: In a test of queued work, set faults by what they apply to, not by when they are set.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** During a rebase the Letta adapter committed onto the detached HEAD, and `git rebase --abort` then dropped the item the plane had recorded as stored (review finding R2-2, critical). Consequence: A plane write during such an operation is a store failure with an event, not a silent loss.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** The first `make test` after the letta doctor check landed failed one test, "doctor and write print no part of the secret", at the 30 s timeout; a rerun passed (91 files, 1903 tests). Consequence: The test is load-sensitive on Windows; if it times out again, the fix is a per-test timeout or fewer doctor runs in it, in its own change.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** An inherited git environment redirected the Letta adapter's git calls: with `GIT_INDEX_FILE` set, a write staged into a foreign index (review finding R2-4). Consequence: None beyond the fix.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** `make test` was red on PR #20: "doctor and write print no part of the secret" ran a full doctor per URL case, about 24 s alone against the 30 s limit (review finding R2-5). Consequence: Tests about something other than the stores pass cheap probes.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** Three smaller defects (review findings R2-8, R2-11, R2-12): an empty folder inside another repository was initialised as a nested repository though ADR 0028 said it was refused; deleting an item file never committed failed with "pathspec did not match"; the 60 s stale age let a live Letta writer's lock be broken, since a write may take several git calls of up to 60 s each. Consequence: None beyond the fixes.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** The first R2-11 test (delete an item file never committed) did not catch its own mutation: putting the never-committed path back into the pathspec left it green. Consequence: Each R2 fix was mutation-checked against the test that names it.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** The race test written for review finding R2-1 accepted any number of items deleted by user commits as git's stale-index race. Consequence: A tolerance in a test is bounded by the evidence for it.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** The first runs of the new race test failed twice for reasons other than R2-1: once an intent-to-add entry stayed in the index after a refused write, and once the user's commit deleted an item Engram had just committed. Consequence: The residual race is documented, not hidden.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** The first conformance run of the Letta adapter failed all 42 of its cases with `TypeError: realpath.native is not a function`. Consequence: None beyond the fix; recorded so the next adapter on `fs/promises` does not repeat it.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** A failed restore after a refused commit was not reported, and the refused item could stay staged (review finding R2-3). Consequence: None beyond the fix.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** The first version of the commit-count test failed: it required every commit to touch both the item file and `engram/MEMORY.md`. Consequence: "Touching only the item file and the index" means at most those two paths; ADR 0028's amendments say so.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** Three mutations of the Letta adapter survived its tests: the pathspec dropped from `git commit`, `--force` dropped from `git add`, and the per-command `core.safecrlf=false` removed (review finding R2-6). Consequence: None beyond the tests.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** The Letta adapter reported writes as stored that were never committed by Engram (review finding R2-1, critical). Consequence: Engram never stages an item's content where another client can commit it.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** Tests relied on a global git identity; Linux CI has none: six Letta tests failed on PR 2's CI with "Author identity unknown". Consequence: Tests that commit name their own identity.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** Two tool problems while working card A2 on Windows: a Python edit rewrote `packages/cli/test/doctor.test.ts` with CRLF line ends, and the worktree guard refused shell commands whose text named git outside a plain form. Consequence: Agents on this machine edit files through the Edit tool or Python in binary mode, never Python text mode.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** Two key-validation tests of the Letta adapter failed on their first run: an upper-case item id and an upper-case ref key were accepted. Consequence: Any test of case rules on ids needs an id with letters in it.
- **2026-10-29, A2, failure.** `engram write --store letta --root <new folder> --db "postgres://u:p@[bad/db"` exited 2 for the bad `--db` but left a git-initialised folder behind (review finding I-1). Consequence: A surface's usage errors leave every store untouched.
- **2026-10-29, B1, failure.** GateMem's cassettes were copied into `fixtures/` by hand, against AGENTS.md's rule that fixtures are regenerated only by `make fixtures`: the 20 answer cassettes on main (PR #6) and the 24 judge cassettes in this PR (review finding P6-3). Consequence: No cassette under `fixtures/` is placed by hand.
- **2026-10-29, B1, failure.** Recording the judge cassettes the first time created an empty `fixtures/bench/gatemem/judge-cassettes` folder, and copying the recorded ones then nested them one level down, so the offline replay missed every judge key. Consequence: The read-only rule of finding R6-5 also covers a committed cassette folder that does not exist yet.
- **2026-10-29, B1, failure.** Two mutants of the judge's fallback parser survived the first golden file: letting `^` or `$` under MULTILINE also break lines at `\r` and U+2028, as JavaScript's `m` flag does, changed no golden case, and a judge prompt filled with `JSON.stringify` in place of Python's `json.dumps` passed too. Consequence: None beyond the added cases.
- **2026-10-29, B1, failure.** The judge port recorded two errors that must stop it as failed attempts (review findings P6-1, P6-2): `piModelCall`'s refusal of a structured-output request, and a judge reply holding a Python literal the port cannot represent (bytes, a set, a complex number). Consequence: A live judge cannot silently drop its schema.
- **2026-10-29, B2, failure.** MEM-INV-Bench's row was first added to the pins table itself, and `make test` failed: `test_the_six_benchmarks_are_the_pinned_ones` holds that table to the six benchmarks the pre-registration registers. Consequence: If the poisoning arm moves to MEM-INV-Bench at Gate B, a pre-registration amendment comes first and the row then moves up.
- **2026-10-29, B3, failure.** Moving `turnTime` into `@engram/bench` and re-exporting it from the runner as `export const turnTime = gatemem.turnTime` made every runner test file fail to load. Consequence: A runner module may use `gatemem.*` only inside functions, never at module top level, while the cycle exists.
- **2026-10-29, B3, failure.** In the B3 review fix pass, the first try at adding the R5-6, R5-7 and R5-10 tests failed to run. The edit was a Python script inside a Bash heredoc, and the heredoc broke on the quotes in the embedded TypeScript. Consequence: No inline heredocs with code in them. Write code to a file first.
- **2026-10-29, B3, failure.** Two shell commands of the coding agent were refused by its worktree guard: a heredoc writing a survey script and a `sed` with a path in a shell variable. Consequence: Agents in worktrees write scripts as files and pass paths literally, as the brief's pitfalls already advise for heredocs.
- **2026-10-29, B3, failure.** The first `make test` after adding the Letta store to the B3 config failed two runner tests at their bounds: the B3 acceptance (120 s) and "the command replays under ENGRAM_OFFLINE=1" (120 s). Consequence: Tests that run the B3 config for something other than the stores avoid the Letta sessions.
- **2026-10-29, B3, failure, supersedes 2026-10-29-a2-runner-letta-store.** The runner's Letta store left its temporary MemFS repositories behind: one `make test` run left 25 `engram-runner-letta-*` folders in the temporary folder. The earlier entry said they were removed when the process exits; they were not. Consequence: This entry replaces the earlier one's cleanup claim; the rest of that entry stands.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure, H1.** The first B4 write-up cited the probe's HIT under C5 as evidence that the plane does not stop the attack. Consequence: Pipeline configs with scripted models report plane decisions and what reached the model, never verdicts, as evidence about the plane.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure.** The first write phase gave every MEM-INV-Bench entry confidence 0.7, and under `strict` the plane denied every team entry below the 0.8 floor. Consequence: Any constant the harness invents for a benchmark is checked against every preset's thresholds before it is used.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure.** B4's first doctor change checked a manifest's `files` and then skipped its `selection`, and hashed only `cases.jsonl` of the MEM-INV-Bench fixture. Consequence: Doctor covers every vendored file of the fixture.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure.** B4's hidden-label tests could not catch a leak of `family` or of the case id, and the case ids encoded family and channel in blocks. Consequence: A stripping test varies every hidden field, including the ones the schema ties together.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure.** During the fix pass a mutation check restored `mem-inv-probe.ts` with `git checkout`, which also discarded that file's uncommitted fix edits. Consequence: Mutations are undone by reversing the mutation itself, or run only on a committed tree.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure.** A B4 test expected the action-time `Plane.read` to return a case's memory in upstream's write order, and failed. Consequence: Order of memory in the prompt is a difference between the plane's conditions and upstream's protocol that a live run should report.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure.** The first run of the MEM-INV-Bench vendoring script stopped with a KeyError on `channel` while reading the recorded study's transcripts. Consequence: Nothing further; the script still refuses to write a case it cannot match to a recorded request.
- **2026-10-29, B4, failure.** Several shell commands of the B4 agent were refused by the worktree guard before running: a heredoc that wrote a Python edit script, an append by heredoc to a TypeScript file, and `git -C .` in a chained command. Consequence: Agents in worktrees write files with the editing tools and run git plainly from the worktree, as the agent brief already advises for heredocs.
- **2026-10-29, E0.4, failure.** The first doctor check for the files adapter passed on a store root that does not exist. Consequence: Nothing further; the store check and the adapter check now agree on a missing root.
- **2026-10-29, E0.4, failure.** Two missteps while building the provider key list and `make test-e2e`: the generator could not read pi-ai's version, and a `git checkout -- Makefile` undid the Makefile edits. Consequence: Probe edits for a red test are made on a copy, not on a tracked file that holds other edits.
- **2026-10-29, E0.4, failure.** The first Postgres wiring of E0.4 leaked secrets in three ways, and its redaction tests could not have caught them. Consequence: A redaction test must exercise a path where a secret really reaches a message.
- **2026-10-29, E1.1, failure.** The itemByStoreRef replay property failed on Postgres on its own generator check, not on the backend. Consequence: A property that also checks its generator states the check per run, so a backend with fewer runs does not fail it for the wrong reason.
- **2026-10-29, E1.1, failure.** The first Postgres backend let a transaction half-commit after its connection dropped, and let a timed-out writer keep the log's advisory lock. Consequence: A backend test suite needs fault cases (dropped connection, failed begin, timeout in a transaction), not only the happy contract.
- **2026-10-29, E2.4, failure.** Three things failed on the way. Invariant 1's read-time generator spread a ttl's end instant, a string, into its characters, so reads ran at times like "6" and the Plane refused them. Its new guard then met no item expiring exactly at the read time. And the first queue test passed with retain outside the queue: its sweep had nothing to do whichever ran first. Consequence: Nothing.
- **2026-10-29, E2.4, failure.** PR 4 passed every gate and its mutation checks, yet the independent review found five important defects: utility eviction of the write that just landed, expired items holding budget slots, retired content plantable by writing an item as retired, eviction reasons leaking to non-readers, and irreversible ttl retirement of shared scopes by any member's clock. On the way to fixing them, the manual-ask check first queried a `policy_id` column the events table does not have. Consequence: Adversarial probes (a hostile member, a wrong clock, a full scope) belong in the acceptance tests of every policy card.
- **2026-10-29, E2.5, failure.** Rebasing card E2.5 onto PR 4's fix pass left merge-conflict markers in docs/conventions/testing.md, a lead-owned file, and they were committed. Consequence: A rebase that leaves markers fails the docs gate.
- **2026-10-29, E2.5, failure.** After the P5 fixes, invariant 2's promotion guards were not met: one landing by confirmation in 1000 cases, and no duplicate or same-actor refusal. Consequence: None beyond the generator change.
- **2026-10-29, E2.5, failure.** The first version of the promotion generator in invariant 2 failed on its fourth case, and its guards were met only a handful of times. Consequence: Nothing beyond the fix; the generator stays.
- **2026-10-29, E8.1, failure.** Tooling failures on the way: inline Python and heredoc edits broke on quoting or were refused by the worktree guard, the mutation script crashed printing test output on the Windows console encoding, and the first no-store test used the fake store and so wrote successfully. Consequence: Mutation checks were rerun with the fixed script; each verdict in the PR is from that rerun.
- **2026-10-29, E8.2, failure.** Two failures while moving the CLI onto the client: the old test that `serve` exits 2 started a real server on port 7313 and waited its 30 s timeout, and `engram retain --owner dana` refused to run because `--owner` opened a files store that needs `--root`. Consequence: None beyond the fixes.
- **2026-10-29, E8.3, failure.** The first `make api` generator emptied `sdk/ts` before writing it and so deleted the `node_modules` link pnpm had made there. Consequence: `make api` can run after `pnpm install` in any order.
- **2026-10-29, E8.3, failure.** openapi-typescript rendered the recursive `JsonValue` component as a property type that names itself, which TypeScript rejects (TS2502). Consequence: Clients see structured item content as `unknown` and narrow it themselves.
- **2026-10-29, E8.5, failure.** The first test for the colon rule (review R4-10) passed with the rule removed. Consequence: A path rule's test names a file that exists without the rule.
- **2026-10-29, E8.5, failure.** After rebasing PR 4 onto PR 3's final branch (`6f8bf66`), `make lint` failed: the UI's view test built a `ReadResponse` without the `policy` field PR 3 added to the read route. Consequence: None; the view logic does not read `policy`.
- **2026-10-29, E8.5, failure.** The Playwright test's skip reason, written with `console.warn` at module level, never reached the terminal when Chromium was missing. Consequence: None beyond the test.
- **2026-10-29, E8.5, failure.** The first e2e step for review R4-4 failed with the fix in place: it saw the old event detail after switching the viewer. Consequence: An e2e step after a viewer change waits on the new viewer's own lanes read.
- **2026-10-29, E8.6, failure.** The first fixture workload let a parser that swaps two filtered categories pass the Playwright test. Consequence: Fixture counts that a test compares by category are kept distinct.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** The R3-2 fix guarded the protected files but not the folders that hold them, so deleting or moving a whole folder (`rm -rf ~/.engram`, `rm -rf .claude`, `mv .claude x`) removed the audit log or the hook unchecked. Consequence: A guard over a file is tested against its folder as well as the file.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** Two failures in the fix pass: a doc comment quoting the glob `memor*/a.md` ended the comment at `*/` and broke compilation, and several inline Python edits were refused by the worktree shell guard. Consequence: Globs in comments are written without `*/`.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** The narrowed commit-message exemption of R3-19 to R3-22 still had three literal holes: an unquoted newline did not end a segment (R3-23), a git alias could evaluate the message (R3-24), and index, stage and stash `rev:path` forms hid a path (`:memory/a.md`, `:0:memory/a.md`, `stash@{0}:memory/a.md`, R3-25). Consequence: An exemption from a security check is built from what is safe by construction, not by excluding known attacks.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** The R3-26 heredoc rule recognised only delimiters starting with a letter, a digit or `_`, so `<<!`, `<<.` and `<<$X` heredoc bodies kept the message exemption and, as a real bash run confirmed, read the store (finding R3-27). Consequence: Where a safe rule and a precise rule differ, take the safe one.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** The version-control message exemption of R3-16 let three kinds of literal bypass through: a command substitution inside a message (`git commit -m "$(cd memory; cat a.md)"`, backticks, `hg`), a `git` word outside command position (`echo git -m "..." | bash`, `bash -c 'eval "$3"' _ git -m "..."`), and a store file read from history (`git show HEAD:memory/a.md`). Consequence: An exemption is tested at its boundary, not only at its intended use.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** The first fix for review finding R3-15 (a quoted argument is one word) let `bash -c "cd memory && cat a.md"` through: the script was read as a quoted argument, and only its path-like inner words were checked. Consequence: ADR 0014's amendment no longer lists the case as passing.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** The single-quoted message exemption of R3-23 to R3-25 still let a command substitution run when an outer shell expanded first: inside a double-quoted `bash -c "..."` or `sh -c "..."` script, and in the body of a heredoc with an unquoted delimiter (`cat <<EOF`, also piped to bash). The verifier confirmed with a real shell that these read the store. Consequence: A quoting argument is judged across every shell that reads the text, not only the last.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** The R3-15 rule, which checked only path-like words inside a quoted argument, reopened literal bypasses that named a root inside a quoted script: `eval "cd memory && cat a.md"`, `bash <<< "..."`, `echo "..." | bash`, `bash -c -- "..."`, `iex "..."`, `python -c "...chdir('memory')"`, `node -e "..."`. Consequence: `grep -rn "memory leak" src` is refused again, which ADR 0014 lists as a kept cost.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** Two mutation checks first survived: removing `notebook_path` from the path keys, and removing the CLI's input-shape check. Consequence: Mutation checks are run against the layer that decides, not a redundant one.
- **2026-10-29, E8.4, failure.** Windows-only test assumption, caught by Linux CI: the hook test `PowerShell Get-Content memory\a.md` passed on the Windows build machine and failed on CI's Linux, because the check resolved the word with the platform's path module, where only Windows reads `\` as a separator. Consequence: Path checks get a posix-path test beside the native one.
- **2026-10-29, E1.1, failure.** From PR 2 (E1.1, merged as #10 on 2026-10-29) until this hotfix, a failing test in the core or cli vitest projects could exit 0, so `make test` and CI could not fail on them. Consequence: A regression test now asserts the exit code of a failing run, not only its output.
- **2026-10-29, failure.** The hotfix's regression test failed on CI (PR #14) although the exit codes it checks were right. Consequence: A test that reads another process's output sets that process's colour environment.
- **2026-10-29, PX1, failure.** Two of the round-2 mutants survived the first set of tests: dropping the `$'...'` decoding in bash.ts, and dropping the tag defusing for non-message entries in the branch-summary handler. Consequence: Nothing further; the mutation list is in the round-2 report.
- **2026-10-29, RN1, failure.** The first version of the tag-shape test compared the three conditions' rendered reads byte for byte after removing the read id and the rules, and failed on the item ids alone. Consequence: None.
- **2026-10-29, S0.3, failure.** Each test project now has a vitest.config.ts taking 30 s per test and 60 s per hook from vitest.shared.ts, in place of vitest's 5 s and 10 s. Consequence: A slow machine no longer fails the suite at random; a hanging test still fails.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure.** The first test comparing the paired bootstrap's BCa interval with scipy's, within Monte Carlo error, failed on a sample of thirds. Consequence: None for the code.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure.** The formatting gate (`pnpm exec biome check --write .`) rewrote the two results files already generated, changing the bytes their sha256 would pin. Consequence: Results files are formatted only by `research/scripts/results_io.py`.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** The first run of the reach tests failed twice on wrong assumptions about the GateMem fixture, not on the code. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure.** A mutation that made z0 count ties as below survived the test suite, and the cause was that floating-point rounding hid the ties altogether. Consequence: Any later statistic comparing resample means with a bound must use `at_or_below` and `at_or_above`.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure.** Tooling failures on the way: a Bash heredoc holding a Python edit broke on its quotes; the worktree guard refused several compound shell commands (loops over files, `git` after a pipe, a variable as a `sed` operand); and the mutation-check script crashed decoding vitest's output in the Windows console code page. Consequence: None for the code.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding V5: the audit sample drew matches, not checkpoints, so a hand audit could not estimate the share of reachable checkpoints whose match is the secret. Consequence: None beyond the result.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding V4: the reach finding said the share whose secret is in reach "lies between" the every-pattern and any-pattern shares; it need not. Consequence: The hand audit of the sample is what can place the secret's share.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V4: round-one entries called the TOST "a TOST that holds its level" without saying which level, and said a betting interval needs "about twice the items" without evidence. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding V4: the refusal finding implied bare words are an access-checkpoint problem of `not_include`, and that the leak-targets-only columns isolate generic patterns; neither holds. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V4: the exact-route threshold entry said a probe "saw no size above 0.05", while the round-one result itself showed measured levels of 0.055 to 0.066 at reported sizes. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V3: H4's input to Holm was undefined. Consequence: PR 4 can recommend H4's alpha knowing Holm.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V2: the round-one level gate admitted true levels above 0.05 and was decided by Monte Carlo noise. Consequence: The round-one `h4-power.json` is withdrawn.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** The first version of the round-two power tests took over three minutes, and one asserted that the rung below a reported size lacks power, which fails when the level gate set the size. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding V4: the first-pass change entries for the two TypeScript entry points still described them as they were before the review. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V1: the round-one TOST's decision was not monotone in the evidence. Consequence: None beyond the test.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** The first test of the power script's exact-level gate passed even with the gate's exact half removed at its call site. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V6: a round-one test comment said the dense level cases catch a per-side level of 0.05; they did not, the zero-discordance tests did. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-6: the round-one power-model decision said the size scales linearly in kappa. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-7: the round-two finding said the largest measured level, 0.043, was measured at small kappas. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review findings R2-1 and R2-2: the round-two gate let through sizes at which the TOST's level exceeds alpha 0.01. Consequence: The round-two table at alpha 0.01 is withdrawn.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-3: the round-two test of the TOST p-value's scale in Holm could not catch a p-value on the one-sided scale. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-4: round two tested the TOST's monotonicity on one family of samples and two property tests, which a floor switched by dispersion can pass. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** A regeneration of the H4 table ran while the mutation checks rewrote `research/analysis/equivalence.py` in the same worktree, and some of its worker processes imported a mutant. Consequence: None for the result; a mutant run beside a regeneration would have produced a wrong table.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-5: at alpha 0.01 the round-two level gate first ran 4,000 simulations per check, so Monte Carlo noise decided which sizes passed. Consequence: The 4,000-run table was never committed as a result.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding R2-6: the reach script's docstring still said the shares with every and with any pattern in reach bracket the share whose secret is in reach. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** A scratch local search over seven-point nulls reported levels up to 0.0149 at 381 items, 5 points, alpha 0.01, well above the three-point supremum of 0.0121. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-5: the first round-two power run simulated every item and draw, was far too slow at large sizes, and was stopped unfinished. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-8: the test of the multinomial item states against simulating every item used equal rates and kappa 0.5 only, at four standard errors of 1,500 studies, too loose to catch a real error and blind to the unequal-rate path the level uses. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-8: the admission finding left out that under strict the trusted ERP and bank filler also waits as a review candidate. Consequence: The admission measurement and finding are superseded; the result stays on the 24 vendored cases until PR 3's 38-case set merges.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure.** Review finding P2-12: the backlog's reachable-leak row said "done on branch prereg-pr2-research", which goes stale once the branch merges. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1, supersedes 2026-11-02-pr2-decision-excluded-sessions.** Review finding P2-9: an excluded session broke the pairing on the same seed. Consequence: This entry supersedes the excluded-sessions decision of the same day.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding P2-6: the H4 power finding read one family's power as H4's, though H4 needs every pair to pass across stores and across models, on two benchmarks. Consequence: The H4 finding is superseded.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding P2-7: the H4 entries carried wrong numbers, and the ladder could pick a rung on Monte Carlo noise. Consequence: The H4 measurement and finding are superseded; the PR text is corrected.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure.** To stop the looping power run I killed every `python.exe` on the machine by image name, not by process id. Consequence: The coordinator is told, so PR 3's agent can rerun anything that died at about 19:31 on 2026-11-02. Later stops target a process id or a task.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** The mutation check of the rung choice's floor hung instead of failing. Consequence: A test of a search loop guards against looping in every case.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** The first version of the test for pairing on kept seeds could not tell the two pairings apart. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-4: the planted null and the H1 table fixture had no item effect, so pairing sessions instead of seed-averaged items passed every test (mutation M1). Consequence: None beyond the tests.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-3: the reach finding called the located-but-unreachable share an upper bound on what C5 can prevent, without defining the shares. Consequence: The finding is superseded; the hand audit is a human step.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-2: the GateMem reach counted leak targets only, while GateMem's scorer also counts an access checkpoint's not_include patterns as a leak. Consequence: The reach measurement and finding are superseded.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** The first run of the reworked H4 power script never finished: its rung choice looped forever on cells whose level failed at the first rung with enough power. Consequence: None beyond the code.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-5: reading the poisoning arm's lower interval end instead of its upper end (mutation M4) survived every test. Consequence: None beyond the tests.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-10: each bootstrap's seed depended on the contrast's position, so adding another model's sessions changed the primary test. Consequence: None beyond the code.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure.** Review finding P2-11: `docs/conventions/testing.md` still described layer 8 as `research/tests/test_reproduce.py`, cache-only and nightly. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding P2-1: H4's first TOST did not hold its level at the sizes the power table reported. Consequence: Every size in the first `h4-power.json` is withdrawn; the superseding measurement entry gives the new ones.
- **2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Adding an item effect to the H1 fixture made a test fail that asserted C5's Wilson interval contains the planted 5 percent. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Two new tests failed on their first run for reasons the code did not cause: C4's prompt was compared with C3's including each session's temporary working directory, and C4's GateMem read was expected to show the family member's own turn as `allowed`. Consequence: The gate can deny an asker their own words; C4's model sees that verdict, which is upstream's behaviour, not a port error.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** The first clones of GateMem and MEM-INV-Bench at their pins into `.engram/upstream/` checked text files out with CRLF line endings, so their bytes no longer matched the manifests' sha256. Consequence: COUNT reports such data as not pinned instead of counting it.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** The first RAG-Policy golden file was written with CRLF line endings, and git warned on commit that it would replace them. Consequence: None beyond the fix; a golden script writes bytes.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** After the vendoring wrote the full case set, card B4's test of the MEM-INV-Bench manifest failed: it lists the vendored files exactly, and `full/cases.jsonl` was new. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A mutation check that let the RAG-Policy gate's billing role see every topic passed the fixture golden test; a second mutation, in the office projects rule, failed it on five checkpoints. Consequence: A change to `rag-policy.ts` should be rerun against the full set with `check-rag-policy-upstream.ts` before it merges.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** The first full `make test` of this PR failed one of 2,409 tests: step-0 task S0.13's check that every ADR in the index is decided rejected ADR 0031's `proposed` row. Consequence: When the lead accepts ADR 0031, the row's status changes and 0031 leaves the set.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** The worktree's shell guard refused several commands of this PR: a search for upstream data across the disk, a pipeline containing the path `bench/eval`, a heredoc writing five configs, and inline Python run through `uv` with the working directory in a variable. Consequence: Agents in worktrees should write files with the write tool and pass literal paths.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The runner scored GateMem answers with the rule-based scorer only, though GateMem scores A, F and U with its judge and ADR 0031 said they go through it. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** The first `make lint` of the review fix pass failed: biome refused an assignment inside an expression (`const kind = (leaks[k] ??= {...})`) in the new verdict comparison script. Consequence: None.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** C5's read returned items C2 to C4 never saw: core matched granted items on any query term while the fake and Letta stores' search needs every term ("what is the schedule": C2 0 items, C5 103). Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A live run of an experiment config ended with "a pipeline config's numbers are still not results". Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** C2 to C4 showed items in the store's order and C5 in the plane's score order, so the same items reached the model in different orders. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** PR 3 changed `packages/pi-extension/src/tag.ts`, outside the plan's file list, without a decision entry. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** `h1-gatemem` includes the utility checkpoints, beyond H1's stopping rule, without a decision entry. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** C4 showed GateMem's RAG-Policy verdicts, a different policy from the one C5 enforces, so C4 minus C5 measured policy difference as well as mechanism. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** C4's legend named the benchmark and its baseline, an evaluation cue only C4 carried. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Eight MEM-INV-Bench attack cases showed every model the reference model's recorded note, while upstream writes it with the model under test. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** `make test` held the RAG-Policy port to 20 checkpoints only, which a billing-role mutation passed, and a log entry claimed a change to the port fails the golden test. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Before commit d7620e7, a live run of an experiment checked and fetched its data before the live guards ran, so a run refused under ENGRAM_OFFLINE=1 could still download GateMem's files. Consequence: The independent review of PR #25 asked for this entry and the regression test; the fix itself was commit d7620e7.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The RAG-Policy hidden-field test stripped both checkpoints before the gate, so it could not fail; the first fix still passed a gate that read `leak_targets`, because the planted value named nothing the gate matches. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** `checkGateMemData` called a directory the full set when the eight pinned files matched, though the loader also loads any other domain directory beside them. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** `runMemInvMain` in `run-mem-inv.ts` had no caller and no experiment guard, so it could replay or record an experiment config. Consequence: Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** One `make test` run of the round-2 fix pass failed in `b3.test.ts`: its check that every Letta repository a probe opens is removed found a new `engram-runner-letta-*` folder in the system temp directory. Consequence: Two test runs on one machine at once can fail this check; it is card B3's test and is left as it is here.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A mutation check that removed the note's whitespace check passed the tests. Consequence: Found while working the round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A unit test named "the limit applies after the filter" did not catch a plane that cut to the limit before its read filter; only the core property test did. Consequence: Found by the mutation checks of the round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The first version of the core pipeline's property test let only 8 of 300 generated reads return items and the limit cut just 1, so it barely exercised ranking and the cut. Consequence: Found while working the round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The first fix pass's every-term rule left C2 with no memory on 120 of 180 fixture reads and on 15 of 20 checkpoint questions, and on Mem0 its top-20 cap broke the subset on 45 reads (534 extra entries). Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The first fix pass amended ADR 0009, an accepted decision, as if decided, without saying the change was a proposal or updating the architecture's read path. Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A finding said no OpenAI model could be H1's answer model through pi-ai, while pi-ai's OpenRouter provider serves them on the seeded API. Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The first fix pass's live judge sent unseeded calls on the Responses API, contrary to ADR 0017 (finding R7-7). Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Two entries of the first fix pass overrode earlier entries without `supersedes`: the engram verdict one (over the RAG-Policy verdict decision) and the agent-note one (over the full case set's line on recorded notes). Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** ADR 0031 said an item is allowed exactly when C5's read would return it, which ignores the query match and the read limit. Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The note kept surrounding whitespace, treated a whitespace-only reply as a note, and failed the run on a failed call, where upstream strips, falls back and goes on. Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The header of `x1-gatemem.test.ts` and the PR body's Plan and Evidence still described C4 with the RAG-Policy verdict. Consequence: Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Round 2 claimed C4 and C5 differ in mechanism only, while C4 showed C2's top 20 with verdicts and C5 filled 20 slots from admitted items: C4 showed 2,793 allowed items on access control against C5's 8,290. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-3 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** B3's and RN1's cassettes were recorded again under the core pipeline in round 2 without a note in the Gate B record or card B3. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-3 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** `make lint` failed on the round-3 C4 length script: an assignment inside an expression (`const row = (byType[t] ??= {...})`), the same pattern round 2's floor script was fixed for. Consequence: New scripts get `pnpm exec biome check` (lint as well as format) before they are committed.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** CI failed `make lint-docs` and `make thesis` on PR #25 (runs 41502477591 and 41502495431): the B3 reads finding cited `.engram/runs/b3-gatemem-plane/results.jsonl` as evidence, a run output under the ignored `.engram/` directory. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25. Evidence names committed files or a command, never a run output.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Two earlier entries still said the store answers a query with terms (`x1-empty-query-lists-all`, `x1-c4-retrieval-shared`), after round 2 made every condition use core's pipeline. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-3 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Round 2 recorded a failed note call on its first failure, so one transient 502 or 429 in a live run would have written the raw poison into the cassette for every replay, and the live judge gave up on its first transient failure too. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25, before any live run.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Under the core pipeline the read event said "returned 20 of 115 candidates; 0 filtered" and lost the 95 items the read limit cut. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-3 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The retrieval property test built its own plane, so the runner's `coreReadPlane` wiring was untested: dropping its limit passed all 45 tests. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-3 fix pass.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure.** While editing the judge, a `python -I -` command with no script opened Python's interactive prompt, which looped on Windows console errors until the shell's two-minute limit moved it to the background. Consequence: Edits go through a script file or the editor, never a bare interpreter.
- **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The floor test asked only whether C2 returned anything, which any-term matching makes near certain; reversing the ranking passed every runner retrieval test. Consequence: Found by the third review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-3 fix pass.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** The commit that added the audit's `stop_share` wrote it as `0.10` in `research/configs/label-audit.json`, and biome's JSON formatter wants `0.1`, so `make lint` fails on that commit on its own. Consequence: Run the whole `make lint` before committing a config, not the linter of the language just edited.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The blind template's order test checked only that the order differs from the sessions' and is seeded; a ranking that put the condition first, which un-blinds the readers, would have passed (P4V-1). Consequence: Where the expected value is computable, a test computes it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The phase-1 test searched only each context's head for labels, so a label appended after the turns or the memory would have passed (P4V-2). Consequence: A blindness test compares the whole artefact with what may be seen.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** In the review fix pass the sensitivity-table mutant's first pattern matched nothing in `research/analysis/h1.py`, so that run tested the unmutated code; it was reported only after a rewritten pattern was killed, and the first run had no failure entry (P4V-3). Consequence: Check that a mutant applied before reading the run.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** `make h1-table READINGS=.engram/runs/h1-unread.json` would have read the filled template and then overwritten it with a blank one (P4V-4). Consequence: A step that reads a human's work never writes to the same path.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** A table config with `sensitivity` but no `gatemem_primary` silently gave no sensitivity table (P4V-5). Consequence: A config entry the code cannot honour is refused, not ignored.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** MEM-INV-Bench's phase-1 contexts showed each memory entry's channel and derivation, which the model does not see under C2 and C3 (P4V-6). Consequence: "What an agent sees" is checked against the conditions, not the data file.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** `c4-evidence.ts` had two doc comments above `walkLists`, the first a stale copy (P4V-8). Consequence: After extracting a function, read the comments on both sides.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** One of seven mutants of this round's audit and H4 work survived: counting GateMem items both members label otherwise than the expected action as rejected passed every test. Consequence: A rule's exclusions need a test as much as its inclusions.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The table's tests for MGS, the gate-by-action flag, read-before-acting and the sensitivity guard were written before the code, but not run and seen failing before it was written. Consequence: Run each new test red before writing the code it tests.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The verifiers found that v1 promises GateMem's MGS as a secondary outcome, but the H1 table never computed it, and the converter dropped the utility sessions it needs (V-1). Consequence: A promise in the protocol is checked against the table's output keys before review.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** `equivalence.py`'s docstring said H4's items are paired by `paired_item_differences`, which keeps an item that lost a seed; decision sheet 13h takes such items out of H4's test (V-19). Consequence: A recommendation that changes analysis is carried into the analysis code before the tag.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** v1 promises, after ADR 0029, to report whether MEM-INV-Bench sessions read memory before acting; the table reported nothing of it (V-2). Consequence: Same lesson as V-1: every reported quantity v1 names needs a field in the table.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** `node packages/runner/scripts/h1-sessions.ts --help` printed "Unknown option '--help'" (V-20). Consequence: Every script reached from the Makefile answers `--help`.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** `make h1-table GATE_BY_ACTION=1` wrote its sessions and table over the main table's files, so the two readings could not stand side by side, and nothing told a table which reading its sessions came from (V-3). Consequence: A second reading of one run gets its own files and a recorded flag, never the same paths.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The decision sheet's audit stops a benchmark when both members reject more than 10 percent of its items, but the agreement script computed no rejections and no stop flag, and put the stop on the expected action, which decides no violation (V-5). Consequence: A stopping rule lives in code with its threshold in a config, not only in prose.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** One of three mutants of the blind kit survived: with the check of a reply's hash against its key entry switched off, every test passed. Consequence: When two checks guard one property, each needs a test that only it catches.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** The orchestrator asked for the blind readings kit to be committed in part B's worktree, on its branch, from PR 4's head `1de4434`. The agent's harness refused every git command outside the agent's own worktree. Consequence: Work for another agent's branch is handed to an agent working in that worktree, or fast-forwarded afterwards.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** `make test` failed twice on this round's head: `packages/runner/test/b3.test.ts` found an `engram-runner-letta-*` folder in the temp directory that was not there before its run. Every one of its tests passed the first time; the second time one acceptance test failed the same check. Consequence: Run the suite with a private temp folder when other runs share the machine, or the check should look only at its own folders.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The final verifier found that turning `all(...)` into `any(...)` on the `both_no` line of `label_audit_agreement.py`, so that one member's no rejects an item, survived every test (F-1). Consequence: A rule that needs both members is tested where they disagree.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** `main()` in `label_audit_agreement.py` reads `stop_share` from the config, but no test reached `main()`: a hardcoded 0.2 survived (F-2). Consequence: The command a member runs is tested, not only the function under it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The gate-by-action table carried a full `primary`, `holm` and `decision` block shaped like the main table's, marked only by `gate_by_action: true`, so it could be read as deciding H1 (F-6). Consequence: A table that does not decide says so in a field, not by omission.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** `make h1-table GATE_BY_ACTION=1` without `READINGS=` rewrote `.engram/runs/h1-unread.json` and its key, harmless only because both table configs share a seed (F-8). Consequence: A second reading never writes the first reading's files, whether or not their content would match.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The C4 evidence module was written before its vitest file, so its tests were first seen passing, never failing. Consequence: The later deliverables of this PR write the test first.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** The commit that added the H1 pipeline's log entries holds one of 153 words, over the 150-word limit, so `make lint-docs` fails on it. Consequence: Only the intermediate commit fails the lint; the branch's head passes it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The cost table's Python tests were written before `research/scripts/h1_cost.py` but not run until the script existed, so they were never seen failing. Consequence: None for the result; the remaining deliverables run each new test before writing the code.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** PR #26's H4 table checked the TOST's level at 0.05 and 0.01 only, while H4's one p-value can meet Holm at 0.05 / k for any k from 1 to 5. Consequence: A test now fails if the power table changes without the record following it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** PR #26 called a search result an exact level over the composite null: "the TOST's exact level over the composite null" (round-3 finding), "Largest composite-null level" (round-3 measurement), "its exact level over the composite null" (`docs/conventions/testing.md`), "every null distribution the three-seed design allows" (`equivalence.py`, and `power.py`'s `_composite_sup`). Consequence: v1 should call these "the largest level a search found".
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** PR #26's round-3 failure entry said the round-3 finding gives its levels with where they occur, but the round-3 finding quotes no level at all. Consequence: None beyond the superseding entry.
- **2026-11-03, X1, failure, supersedes 2026-11-02-x1r3-failure-mutation-restore-lost-work.** The round-3 mutation script of PR #25 restored each mutated file with `git checkout`, which also discarded the uncommitted C4 walk in `conditions.ts` and `retrieve.ts`. Consequence: Mutation checks in this repository commit first and restore by copy.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** The first `make test-repro` on this branch reproduced 5 of 7 results; `h4_power.py` and `h4_holm_levels.py` exited with 1. Consequence: On a shared machine, run `make test-repro` when no other worker pool is running; a worker-count limit for the two H4 scripts is a possible follow-up.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** Two tooling failures on the way: the mutation runner's first run crashed printing a test's output, and the agent's shell refused several compound commands (a heredoc edit script, a `cd` before `git`, a loop over computed paths). Consequence: None for any result.
- **2026-11-03, X1, failure.** `docs/log/2026-11-02-x1r2-b3-reads.md` was edited in place by PR #25's commit `756096d`, although entries are immutable (documentation convention, rule 5). Consequence: An unmerged entry whose evidence cannot exist may be corrected in place, and the correction is logged like this; a merged entry is superseded.
- **2026-11-03, X1, failure.** PR #25's commit `2b075e4` added `docs/log/2026-11-02-x1r3-read-reason-cut.md` with a body of 152 words, so `make lint-docs` failed on that commit; the next commit, `55ff7fd`, shortened it. Consequence: Run `make lint-docs` on its own, without a pipe, before committing a log entry.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** In the review fix pass, the commit that added the cost entries (message "wip: cost review entries before mutation checks") holds five entries over 150 words, so `make lint-docs` fails on it. Consequence: Committing work before a mutation check does not excuse skipping the docs lint: lint, then commit, then mutate.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The converter counted MEM-INV-Bench's UNREAD and MALFORMED replies as "no violation", and its comment said this was "as upstream's scoring does"; it was not. Consequence: Any scoring claim about upstream cites upstream's code.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** `make test-repro` ran out of memory in the H4 scripts' workers again, for the reviewer as for part A's first run, and part A logged it without a fix. Consequence: On a shared machine, `ENGRAM_RESEARCH_WORKERS=8 make test-repro`.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** Part A's H4 finding quoted sizes from the alpha 0.05 column beside those from 0.01, for example 100 items at 10 points, while only the alpha 0.01 sizes are checked at Holm's lower thresholds. Consequence: Part B takes H4's margin and sample from the alpha 0.01 column.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The cost table's low estimate counted no reasoning tokens for any model, because the runner asks for thinking off; for six candidates that request never reaches the model. Consequence: Read the transport, not only the catalogue's flags.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The first label-audit kit showed members the answer: sheets had a `benchmark_label` column, GateMem contexts opened with the expected action and leak targets, MEM-INV-Bench contexts printed the family and scoring keys, and the agreement read the benchmark label from an editable sheet. Consequence: An audit measuring agreement shows its members nothing to agree with.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The end-to-end test of H1's pipeline passed with a mutated converter that ignored the judge, read `privacy_leak` at forgetting checkpoints and stopped excluding EXCLUDED and FILTERED replies; only the vitest file caught them. Consequence: An end-to-end test plants every branch it claims to cover.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** Part A's report and `PR.md` said Anthropic's models are "served only on `anthropic-messages`"; `anthropic/claude-*-5.5:batch` and `~anthropic/claude-*-latest` are on `openai-completions` in the same catalogue. Consequence: A summary keeps a finding's qualifiers.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The cost table priced `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` at an input price of 0.00576 dollars per million tokens without remarking that it looks wrong. Consequence: A flagged price is checked against the provider's own page before a model is chosen on it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The candidate decision entry said `-pro` variants were excluded while the config's DeepSeek large pick is `deepseek-v4-pro-0813`. Consequence: A selection rule is written from the picks it produced, then checked against them.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The cost table took tokens per memory item, and the judge's prompt, from the two fixture checkpoints unscaled, while it scaled the item count and the probe prompt to the full set. Consequence: Scale by what a session shows, not by a whole-episode mean.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** Part A's commit `68cbbd5` edited two of its own log entries in place, `pr4a-failure-c4-test-after-code` and `pr4a-failure-commit-past-lint-docs`, removing the pre-rebase hashes `cc14a86` and `dc0e139`, and left no note. Consequence: A log entry names no branch hash that a rebase or a squash will remove.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** H1's pipeline could not run the analysis v1 recommends: access-control checkpoints as the primary GateMem set, forgetting as secondary, and a sensitivity table without the checkpoints where a plain refusal is scored as a leak. Consequence: v1's analysis is the tagged commit's.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** Tests timed out under machine load: one of the 11 count tests, whose name I did not capture, and 8 doctor tests at the 30-second limit. Consequence: Gates run one at a time here, and a timeout is rerun alone before it is taken for a fault.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** The worktree's shell guard refused several compound commands: a loop feeding `bc`, a timed subshell, a heredoc writing scratch configs, and a command mixing `git` with other steps. Consequence: Shell steps stay single and plain in this environment, as part A also found (`docs/log/2026-11-03-pr4a-failure-tooling.md`).
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** Item 1 said Anthropic's two current models are on `anthropic-messages`; every Anthropic model in the catalogue is, Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.1 included. Consequence: Item 1 and v1 name the catalogue's models.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-5: the audit's 10 percent rule sat on the benchmark's label, which on GateMem is the expected action, a label no violation depends on. Consequence: D4: the rule sits on GateMem's keys and on MEM-INV-Bench's family and keys.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-13: v1's H4 omitted the level caveat below 761 items and how the items are drawn. Consequence: v1 states both; the draw is fixed in card X9.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** v1's H4 names files, Mem0 and Letta, but the runner's stores are fake, Mem0 and Letta. Consequence: v1, item 12 and card X9 say the files store joins before H4.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-9: item 1 called gpt-5.6-terra a large tier and its C2 comparable with the recorded study. Consequence: Item 1 corrected and reasoning off weighed against instruction following.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-4: item 10 treated GateMem's rule scorer as deciding sessions and offered judging alone as an alternative, but H1's run already judges every GateMem row. Consequence: Item 10 rewritten; the false alternative dropped.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-10: items 13a to 13h had no Question and no "If changed" line. Consequence: Each now has both.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-7: items 11 and 18 did not say that under medium C5's read on MEM-INV-Bench is C2's plus the tag. Consequence: Both items weigh it and add the descriptive alternative.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-14: v1's multiplicity line gave H2 a p at or below zero, which its support rule does not fit. Consequence: Each primary p now follows its Test field.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-20: v1 called the UNREAD readings blind to the condition, and section 7 did not commit the readings' key. Consequence: Reworded; D5 in section 7.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** A rate difference between two conditions was not distinguished from the paired mean the test uses. Consequence: v1's Statistics says so.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-12: item 9 weighed the forgetting set's dilution but not the primary set's, and said the empty checkpoints add no difference. Consequence: Item 9 weighs it and uses v1's wording.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-16: card R1 named the worktree branch, not PR #27 on `prereg-pr4-v1`. Consequence: R1 names the PR.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-17: item 20 gave about 68 percent, another model's share, item 1 called v4.1-flash's total almost all judge, and the small-tier range did not say it left out the flagged model. - **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-11: v1 did not describe the runner's in-process Mem0, and item 2 weighed time but not fidelity. Consequence: v1 and item 2 state it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-15: v1's fixed subsets for M2 and M3 listed MEM-INV-Bench, which no hypothesis runs off M1. Consequence: The line now names no MEM-INV-Bench subset.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-3: v1 said `make h1-table GATE_BY_ACTION=1` gives the wrong-action reading beside the table, but the command rebuilt the primary table in place. Consequence: D2: a separate sessions file, config and table, each carrying the flag.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-1: v1 promised GateMem's MGS as H1's secondary, but no part of the pipeline computed it, and the converter dropped the utility rows MGS needs. Consequence: D1: the table computes MGS per condition in both readings; v1 and item 20 define it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-2: v1 promised MEM-INV-Bench's count of sessions that read memory before acting (ADR 0029), but the table did not report it. Consequence: D3: the table reports `read_before_action` per condition; v1 and section 7 name it.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-6: item 4 stated only that the empty checkpoints pull C4 minus C5 toward zero, and v1 called C4 "the stronger instruction condition". Consequence: Item 4 states both directions; v1's wording no longer presumes which instruction condition leaks less.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** F-4: item 14's Evidence cited three entries that later entries supersede. Consequence: Item 14 cites the chain heads, and a script over every `supersedes` field finds no superseded citation left in the sheet, v1 or section 7.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** F-5: v1 and the sheet defined MGS's U as `utility_ok` alone without saying it is neither upstream's U nor the paper's Eq. 10, nor that it makes the table's MGS differ from the run's `summary.json`. - **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** F-7: item 9 said C5 hands a readable pattern to the model, and several lines called the gate-by-action reading Eq. 11's, though F's action rule is Eq. 12. Consequence: Item 9 says C5 can hand it over; v1, the sheet and section 7 name Eqs. 11 and 12.
- **2026-11-03, R1, failure.** `make test` on part B's last-polish head failed twice on tests that pass alone: first two Letta adapter tests timed out at 30 seconds and a temporary MemFS folder could not be removed (EPERM); then `packages/runner/test/b3.test.ts` found another session's `engram-runner-letta-*` folder in the shared temporary directory. - **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** P4V-7 of the final verification: section 7 and item 17 had the PR that writes the answers rewrite only PR 4's new MANIFEST rows, though PR 4 also changed the code behind the reach, H4 power, C4 walk and agreement rows. - **2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** V-18 of the final verification: the sheet did not name the departure from the GateMem paper's Eq. 11, which counts a wrong action in A, and v1 did not say where that reading appears. - **2026-11-06, E8.9, failure.** The workload shape card E0.3 declared, the events of a run, could not carry the simulation card E8.9 asks for. Consequence: The interface stubs of E0.3 for lint and distill should be checked against their cards before L1 and D2 build on them.
- **2026-11-06, E8.9, failure.** The self-review before opening the PR found that a workload's promote steps took any string as an item id and a target scope, and its retain steps any string as a scope. Consequence: None for valid workloads; the OpenAPI document names the formats.
- **2026-11-06, L1, failure.** `make test` failed before any L1 change and still fails after it, in tests L1 does not touch. Consequence: The path bug needs its own fix (decode the URL with `fileURLToPath`); it is reported, not silenced, and out of this card's scope.
- **2026-11-06, L1, failure.** The first run of `engram lint` failed with a TypeScript syntax error in packages/cli/src/commands.ts. Consequence: Edits to source go through the editor, not through generated string replacement.
- **2026-11-06, L1, failure.** The first fault workspace was refused by `engram lint`: the policy loader reported no read matrix row for role and org. Consequence: Fixture policies are written to pass the loader, since the CLI reads them.

- **2026-11-06, PX2, failure.** The first replay design loaded the bundled preset a session's policy id named, and the E2.4 session failed to replay: three events differed. - **2026-11-06, PX3, failure.** `uv sync` in a fresh worktree failed with "Missing expected target directory for Python minor version link" for the managed CPython 3.12.16. Consequence: Nothing for the code. A member who meets the error can point `UV_PYTHON` at the managed interpreter.
- **2026-11-06, failure.** Adding `external` to the item provenance generator broke the core read pipeline property's coverage check: no read in its seeded runs hit the read limit. Consequence: A new enum value goes into a separate arbitrary, not an existing one.

- **2026-11-06, failure.** `make test` on clean main (9a0d185) failed on a Windows 11 laptop with the checkout at a path holding a space (`D:\Graduation Project\engram`), before any change for step 1. - **2026-11-07, failure.** `packages/core/test/unit/schema.test.ts` and `packages/runner/test/retrieval.test.ts` failed to load on a checkout whose path holds a space (`D:\Graduation Project\engram`), with ENOENT on `D:\Graduation%20Project\...`. - **2026-11-07, L1, failure.** A security review of card L1 found that `Plane.lint` admitted any member of the scope, while `Plane.read` also applies the policy's read matrix (`readableScope`). Consequence: Lint findings show only what the caller may read.
- **2026-11-13, B5, failure.** After main (schema version 1, PR #29) reached the B5 branch through the updated `@engram/model` branch, the offline B5 replay failed on its first C5 call: no cassette for its key. Consequence: No cassette was re-recorded; PR #37 merges after PR #36.

- **2026-11-13, failure.** When the store failed to delete an item during offboarding (card E9.7), the plane still erased it from the projection and the log under a reason saying "deleted from the store", and adoption (`adoptStoreItems`, card E8.2) then re-imported its plaintext. - **2026-11-19, RV1, failure.** The first spend-ceiling tests failed for a reason other than the code under test: the run never stopped. Consequence: A test that needs a call's cost sets it after the faux provider answers, as `scriptedAnswers` already zeroes it there.
- **2026-11-19, RV1, failure, H1.** The review of PR #55 (RV1-1, blocking) found that a judge or lint call failing for a transient reason after its retry was written into the cassette, so every resume replayed the failure. Consequence: A transient outage never fixes a verdict.

- **2026-11-19, RV1, failure, H1.** The review (RV1-3) found that the usage ledger left out retried attempts and calls that failed for good, so the spend ceiling saw less than was paid. Consequence: A ledger's paid calls are its provider calls, so `calls.new` counts failed attempts too.

Consequence: The scale test now holds the reader `make h1-table` runs.
- **2026-11-19, RV1, failure, H1.** The review (RV1-6) found that any transient outage longer than about 3 seconds stopped a live run, which then needed `RESUME=1`, and that `Retry-After` is not honoured. Consequence: A live run stops only after about 12 minutes of outage.
- **2026-11-19, RV1, failure.** The review (RV1-7) found that the run's fingerprint left out `data` and `script`, so resuming a pipeline config after its script changed would mix two runs. Consequence: A resume refuses a folder written with another script or data set.

- **2026-11-20, RV6, failure, H1.** A pinned and an unpinned live run of one config and model shared the default live cassette folder, so a fresh `LIVE=1` under a new pin replayed answers another upstream gave (review P60-4). - **2026-11-20, RV6, failure, H1.** The lock took its holder's start as Date.now() minus ps etimes, at one second resolution (verifier finding P60-R2). ## Govern

**2026-11-21, E2.11, finding.** On the HTTP API the provenance source of a write is self-asserted: the server passes the request body's actor, source included, to `Plane.write`. The review of PR #69 checked ADR 0040's claim that every surface sets its source. The CLI, the MCP server, the Claude Code hook and the pi extension set a source themselves. The write route in `packages/server/src/routes.ts` does not, so an HTTP client may send no source or `source: user`. ADR 0040's floor covers the first case only. The registered runs use the plane in process through the pi extension, which sets the source. ADR 0040 states the limit, card E2.11 writes it into `docs/architecture.md`, and card E8.12's ADR maps a token to an actor and a source and ignores a client-sent source.[^1][^2][^3]

**2026-11-21, E2.7, finding.** Of the two trust gaps the GitHub sweep reported in Engram's own code, the first is documented behaviour and the second is unconfirmed. The plan was about to call both suspected bugs; the code was read before the cards were written. `withPlaneProvenance` in `packages/core/src/plane/write.ts` lets a caller's claimed provenance stand when the actor has no source: that is ADR 0004's decision D4, stated in the function's comment. Whether it is a gap depends on which surfaces can reach the plane with no source, which nobody has listed. The eviction and rewrite claim (sweep finding GH-38) was found by reading and has not been run. Card E2.7 tests both and lists the surfaces before card E2.11 changes anything. ADR 0040 is worded as an amendment to decision D4, not a bug fix.[^4][^5][^6][^7]

**2026-11-21, R4, failure.** The first crosswalk stated more than it showed in four ways: the Omnibus statement, a claim contradicted by the code (C44), links without their tension, and claims half evidenced. "Names none of the cited articles" skipped Article 42(3), added by point (18), which bears on Article 15. C44 said core reads no clock; the Postgres transport does. C27 cited "time limits for erasure" for a ttl that keeps the bytes; C05 and C27 omitted the tension C26 declares; C17 called a grant what a record of consent needs. C16, C17, C26 and C43 named tests for one half of their claim; C29's test asserted only allows. Each is fixed in the fix pass and the README says a grant is an access permission, not consent. The review's own probe of the wording ban found it evaded; the ban is now whole-word and tested.[^8][^9]
