With a fresh copy per cell, the files adapter's read median is 97 to 108 ms at 100 items and 0.85 to 1.06 s at 1,000, against 3.7 to 5.0 ms and 11 to 23 ms on the fake (`research/results/h2-perf-budget.json`); presets now differ by at most 1.25 times on it. Allowed writes cost 8 to 23 ms at the median, 1.0 to 2.4 times the fake's. Not measured at 10,000 items; an index is a store-side change outside this card.[^279][^280]

**2026-11-21, X15, finding, supersedes 2026-11-21-x15-finding-linear-projection-scans.** Every Plane call reads whole tables while its statement count stays fixed, and an allowed write also reads the whole read history (review finding X15-2). The first entry named only the projection scans. Rows read, now counted, at N items: an allowed write 3N+5 (`retiredContent`, `enforceBudget`, `retentionUsage` each read the projection), a denied write N+2, a read 2N+2; `count.test.ts` asserts this at 100 and 2,000 items. An allowed write also parses every read event (`retentionUsage`): 50 earlier reads add exactly 50 rows to it, a test pinned under X15-2; the review timed 0.88 ms rising to 9.16 ms after 2,000 reads at 100 items. A read also hashes every reachable item three times (`strictContentHash`). A scoped query fixes neither for a reader in every scope. Two backlog rows; the read-history scan is a production scaling problem.[^281][^282][^283][^284][^285]

**2026-11-22, E2.13, finding, supersedes 2026-11-22-e2-13-finding-everything-the-answer-reveals.** The fields each asker is shown, corrected by the verification (finding E2.13-7). The superseded entry said every answer shows matrix entries, and omitted the owner's closed reason. - Refused: the id or scope it gave, and `at`; an unknown id gets the same. - Answered, open item: matrix entries (scope, level, rule id). A closed item lists no readers. - Admitted grantee or member, open item: the owner id and each live grant naming it, without position. - Member, closed item: that it is closed and the rule id; no owner, instant or grant. - Owner: every grant with position, dormant grants with instants, and its closed reason with the item's own instants. - Scope answer: its matrix entries. `at` appears on every read event a reader gets, so it adds nothing.[^286][^287]

**2026-11-22, E8.14, finding.** A text read's `filtered` count may reveal whether an item reached only through a grant that does not admit it contains the query's words; recorded for the lead, not changed. Found by the review of card E8.14. `Plane.read` matches term shares over `reach`, and `reachable` counts any grant whose `to` matches the reader, whatever its class or expiry; an item so reached that holds a query term is a candidate, which the filter removes and `filtered` counts. A reader holding an expired or wrong-class grant to an item can ask word by word and see `filtered` move. Decision D5 allows such items to count in `filtered`; the by-id read now drops them (ADR 0047). `Plane.read` is registered behaviour, so it is left as it is. For the lead: whether a text read should drop such candidates too, after the tag.[^288][^289]

**2026-11-22, E8.15, finding.** The plane has no read-only preview of a retention sweep, so the TUI cannot say before a read how many items the owner's sweep would retire. Review improvement 8 asked the read dialog to show the expired count. `POST /retain` sweeps for real, and `POST /read` runs the owner's sweep before reading; neither has a dry run, and the TUI may not compute one itself (AGENTS.md rule 1). The read dialog of section 8.5 says that any expired item will be retired, without a number, and the design names a sweep preview as a backlog item for the plane. A later plane card could add a preview route; the dialog would then show its count with no other change.[^290][^291]

**2026-11-22, L6, finding, supersedes 2026-11-21-l6-finding-model-close-provenance.** What an agent's close does to a user-written fact depends on the preset. The review (L6-4) found the earlier finding incomplete: it described only medium. On medium-trust, the agent's close through memory_supersede is denied under engram/write/downgrade, and the owner's own close as a user is allowed. On the registered medium, the agent's close is allowed and the fact's provenance becomes kind model. Over HTTP with credentials, the credential's source is the writer's (E8.12), so these hold for that source. Three MCP tests cover the first two. Under medium an agent can turn a person's fact into a model-written one by closing it. The lead may want medium-trust for agents, or a close that keeps provenance; the latter is a write-path change.[^292][^293]

**2026-11-25, L8, finding, H3.** What would change if the registered call sites dropped `reach: "scope-registered"` after the tag. The lead decides whether H3 moves to the filtered reach. The H3 MANIFEST rows would not change: under the default, h3-fault-set, h3-lint-floor, h3-near-duplicate and h3-lint-nli reproduced their sha256. The changes would fall on near-duplicate-presets.test.ts's pinned report hashes: under each of the strict and medium presets, 21 of 34 fixture runs change. Most change only in wording: an orphan reads "names no active item you can reach" in place of "names an item retired by engram/retention/item-ttl". The (lint, item) findings change in orphan.flag.json under both presets, where the source-changed finding on a retired source goes, and in base state dfde3500 under strict. Collapsing to one reach later means re-pinning that test's four hashes, and nothing in research/results.[^294][^295]

**2026-11-25, failure.** The first captures drew the workspace folder's absolute path, which named this machine's user and temp folder. It appeared in the write result's log line, Doctor, the Serve start line, the MCP snippet and line mode. The CLI prints the SQLite log's resolved path, and the MCP snippet makes every path absolute on purpose (design 8.15). The frames therefore differed by machine and could not be compared byte for byte. The script now shows the workspace folder as `~/demo`, as the design's mockups do. It changes only the printed text of each result, never the call that was made, and it does so before the TUI wraps the text. screens.test.ts asserts that no frame names the folder. index.md says that paths are shown as ~/demo.[^296][^297]

**2026-11-27, RD1, finding.** Explanations stored before this card merges keep the stored place of each grant: `explain` on an older read in an existing log can still return `grants[1]:person:eve` to eve (review finding 7). `grants_used` is renumbered when a read stores its explanations (`plane/read.ts`), so only reads made after the merge carry the shown numbering. Renumbering in `storedExplanations` on return would need the item as it stood at that read, and a grant change since would renumber a past answer differently from what the reader was given. Not changed. The leak is the index alone, only to the reader that made the read (explain is maker-only), and only for logs written before the merge: the team's dogfood logs. If the lead wants it closed, a follow-up renumbers on return against the read's own time; nothing registered reads `grants_used`.[^298][^299]

## Research

**2026-10-22, S0.6, finding, H1.** MPBench (arXiv 2606.04329) has no code or data release; it is an AIWILD workshop paper at ICML 2026, not a main-conference paper. The poisoning arm of ADR 0015 depends on it. Searched the abstract, PDF links, ICML page, GitHub and Hugging Face; found only two empty dataset repositories under accounts named after two authors. The judge model is not named in the paper. A request to the corresponding author is drafted, not sent. Card BF1 reconstructs the probe format; MEM-INV-Bench is the alternative.[^300][^301][^302]

**2026-10-25, RN1, finding, H1.** GateMem's own query prompt carries a domain access policy (GLOBAL RULES and GLOBAL ACCESS POLICY) for every method, so in the RN1 runner, C2 on GateMem is not policy-free. The pre-registration defines C2 as having no policy text and C3 as the policy in the system prompt; H1 compares them. The runner keeps the upstream prompt in every condition, so the conditions differ only in mechanism. C3 adds the Engram preset's YAML and the requester's memberships on top. The probe also maps each GateMem turn to its speaker's person scope, with no grants from relationships. That mapping is a placeholder, and it decides what C5 admits. Before any H1 run, X1 or an amendment must decide whether C2 drops GateMem's policy block, and how GateMem principals and relationships map to scopes and grants.[^303][^304][^305]

**2026-10-29, B3, finding, H1.** Under ADR 0030's mapping, C5 enforces no deletion, so GateMem's forgetting score measures the model, not the plane. H1's primary test is GateMem on the primary model, C4 minus C5, over the 727 access-control and 763 active-forgetting checkpoints (`docs/research/preregistration.md`). The literature note says "A and F should be zero by construction for a correct plane" (line 31). No agent-visible field carries a deletion, so the plane never retires a deleted value. In the B3 forgetting probe (`ckpt_20`), the deleted staging token reaches the asker's context on fake and Mem0 (`context leak true`). The note's claim fails for F under this mapping. For A it holds only outside what the mapping's relationships reach. A deletion mechanism (a retire tool with model-driven ingestion, or a deletion lint) is backlogged for the lead to decide before the pre-registration leaves the backlog. ADR 0030 cites it.[^306][^307][^308][^309]

**2026-10-29, B3, finding, H1.** What GateMem's agent-visible fields say about sharing, on the full pinned set (91 episodes, fetched by the dry run). ADR 0030's mapping can express only what those fields carry. 85 episodes list relationships, 90 types with no documented semantics; their fields name principals, groups (project, program, case, household, course, building) or free-text restrictions (`access_scope` 275 times, plus `scope`, `access_level`, `service_scope`); one has an `expires` date, and one `guest_id` (`guest_noah_kim`) names a guest who is not a principal. No turn has `record_refs` or `memory_ops`, so deletions exist only as sentences. In the B3 forgetting probe the deleted staging token reaches the Monsoon security engineer's context on both stores (`context leak true`), since relationships, not deletions, set reach. Forgetting checkpoints need a deletion mechanism the mapping lacks; the GateMem arm reports it.[^310][^311][^312]

**2026-10-29, B3, finding.** With the Letta store in the B3 config, the B3 acceptance test (the config run twice offline) takes about 207 s on this Windows machine, against its earlier 120 s bound. Every ingested GateMem turn is one Letta commit: 474 writes per run, each three git processes (a `cat-file` check, an intent-to-add, the commit) at tens of milliseconds each on Windows. The test's bound is now 600 s and the cross-store denial test's 180 s (that test takes about 18 s on Letta). The live-replay test (R5-5, `live.test.ts`) now replays the config on fake and Mem0 only, since it tests the replay, not the store. `make test` on Windows is longer by about three minutes; on Linux git processes cost a few milliseconds, so CI stays within the layer 7 budget. Fewer git calls per write would be an adapter change of its own.[^313][^314][^315]

**2026-10-29, B4, failure, H1.** The first B4 write-up cited the probe's HIT under C5 as evidence that the plane does not stop the attack. With a scripted model the reply is fixed per case: it does not depend on what the plane admitted, so the verdict cannot reflect the plane. Review R6-1 showed it: with web's kind set to `model` the plane denied the poison and a recording run still printed C5 HIT. The finding now rests on the write decisions and on the C5 cassette carrying the poison inside the read's memory tag, which a test asserts. Pipeline configs with scripted models report plane decisions and what reached the model, never verdicts, as evidence about the plane.[^316][^317]

**2026-10-29, B4, finding.** MEM-INV-Bench's bucketer raises TypeError on a reply whose `tool` is a list or an object, where the port buckets it OTHER. Review finding R6-10; upstream tests `a.get("tool") not in TOOLS` against a set, which needs a hashable value. The golden script now records both rows as `raises` (`TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'` and `'dict'`); the port's comment states the difference and a test holds it to OTHER and UNREAD, which never counts as a hit or a refusal. A live run whose model returns such a reply is scored where upstream's run would stop; the live half reports any such rows.[^318][^319][^320]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure.** A mutation that made z0 count ties as below survived the test suite, and the cause was that floating-point rounding hid the ties altogether. On a 30-item sample of thirds, scipy's exact comparison found 189 of the 611 resamples whose mean equals the observed mean in exact arithmetic; this module's multinomial sums found none of 637. The same rounding decided whether a resample at zero counted as "at or below zero", the pre-registered p-value. Comparisons now use a 1e-9 tolerance (decision entry of the same day). New tests check the p-value and the TOST shares against exact integer sums, and z0 on ties split by rounding; the z0, strict-p and no-tolerance mutants are now killed. The first H4 power run, on the old code, was stopped and discarded. Any later statistic comparing resample means with a bound must use `at_or_below` and `at_or_above`.[^321][^322][^323]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding V5: the audit sample drew matches, not checkpoints, so a hand audit could not estimate the share of reachable checkpoints whose match is the secret. Checkpoints with many matching patterns were over-represented, and a checkpoint's other matches were missing beside the one drawn. The sample now draws 25 seeded reachable checkpoints per kind, each with all its patterns and matches; a test checks each sampled checkpoint carries exactly its row's matches. None beyond the result.[^324][^325]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding V4: the reach finding said the share whose secret is in reach "lies between" the every-pattern and any-pattern shares; it need not. A checkpoint whose every pattern is a bare word ("no", "yes", a short token) counts as every-pattern reachable on a coincidence, so the every-pattern share is not a lower bound. The result now counts checkpoints with only bare patterns, and the superseding finding presents the two shares without calling them a bracket. The hand audit of the sample is what can place the secret's share.[^326][^327]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V4: round-one entries called the TOST "a TOST that holds its level" without saying which level, and said a betting interval needs "about twice the items" without evidence. The level was checked only by a noisy gate (finding V2), and the betting figure was a back-of-envelope estimate that no committed script computes. The superseding entries state the level the new TOST is shown to hold, from the result's exact and measured columns, and drop the betting figure. None.[^328][^329]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding V4: the refusal finding implied bare words are an access-checkpoint problem of `not_include`, and that the leak-targets-only columns isolate generic patterns; neither holds. The 12 forgetting checkpoints at which a refusal scores as a leak have leak targets that are literally "no", and forgetting checkpoints carry "yes" or "no" targets too, so the targets-only columns also count coincidental matches. The result now counts yes or no patterns and bare patterns for the leak targets and the scored set separately; the superseding finding states both kinds. None.[^330][^331]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V4: the exact-route threshold entry said a probe "saw no size above 0.05", while the round-one result itself showed measured levels of 0.055 to 0.066 at reported sizes. The scratch probe ran 1,000 runs per case and printed only cases above 0.05 without checking against the committed result; I reported the probe as if it covered the result's sizes. The exact route and its threshold are withdrawn (decision entry `2026-11-02-pr2-r2-decision-no-exact-route.md`); claims about levels now cite the result's own exact and measured columns. None.[^332][^333]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V3: H4's input to Holm was undefined. The round-one TOST compared its p-value with alpha / 2, so passing it to `decide_h1` as H4's primary p-value would have tested H4 at twice the level Holm assigned it. The TOST now reports p = min(1, 2 max(p_lower, p_upper)), and equivalence at level a holds exactly when p < a; a test passes H4's p-value through Holm and `decide_h1` and checks the decision against the TOST run at Holm's threshold. The power table gives sizes at alpha 0.01 beside 0.05, since Holm over five may test H4 at 0.01. PR 4 can recommend H4's alpha knowing Holm.[^334][^335]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V2: the round-one level gate admitted true levels above 0.05 and was decided by Monte Carlo noise. It accepted a measured level up to 0.05 plus two standard errors, so a true level of 0.0548 (381 items, 5 points, computed exactly) passed in one cell and failed in another; and independent-seed cells were checked only under independent seeds, though seeds may repeat their outcome. The gate now requires the exact level at kappa 0, under both seed models, to be at most alpha, and the measured level at kappas 0.02, 0.05, 0.1 and the cell's own, under both seed models, to be at most alpha by two standard errors (decision entry of the same day). A test stubs power and simulation so the exact level alone decides. The round-one `h4-power.json` is withdrawn.[^336][^337]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding V1: the round-one TOST's decision was not monotone in the evidence. It switched from an exact bound to the bootstrap at 10 items differing towards a side, so at 761 items and a 2-point margin, with k items at -1/3 and k at +1/3 (mean 0), it declared equivalence for k up to 7, refused it at 8 and 9, and declared it again from 10; the power table's sizes in sparse cells were set by where that gap closed. The route is gone: one score test decides at every count (decision entry `2026-11-02-pr2-r2-decision-score-tost.md`). Property tests require that removing disagreement at a fixed mean never turns equivalence into a refusal. None beyond the test.[^338][^339]

**2026-11-02, R1, finding, H1, supersedes 2026-11-02-pr2-review-finding-refusal-leaks.** Under GateMem's rule-based scorer a plain refusal is scored as a leak at 29 of 727 access checkpoints and 12 of 763 forgetting ones, through patterns that are bare words. At access checkpoints the words come mostly from `not_include` (48 checkpoints with a yes or no pattern in the scored set, 4 among the leak targets); at forgetting checkpoints they are the leak targets themselves: 12 checkpoints list both "yes" and "no" as targets, so the targets-only columns do not isolate generic patterns either. The second verifier counted 24 such forgetting checkpoints by counting "yes" and "no" separately. No condition can prevent these leaks under the rule-based scorer; whether GPT-4o's judgement counts them is unmeasured (`refusal_leak`, `with_yes_no` in `research/results/h1-gatemem-reach.json`). For the lead: H1's access and forgetting metrics may need bare-word patterns set aside, or the judge alone.[^340][^341]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-6: the round-one power-model decision said the size scales linearly in kappa. It does not: the TOST's variance adds the margin squared and is floored at margin (1 - margin), neither of which scales with kappa. From kappa 0.25 to kappa 1 the size grows 1.40 to 4.00 times at alpha 0.05 in round two's table. Superseded by `pr2-r3-decision-power-model`, which states the ratios from the regenerated table. None.[^342][^343]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-7: the round-two finding said the largest measured level, 0.043, was measured at small kappas. It was measured at the cell's own kappa, 0.25; the sentence summarised the gate's kappa grid without reading where the maximum fell. Superseded by `pr2-r3-finding-h4-power`, whose levels come from the table's own fields, the largest own level with the kappa it occurs at (`own_max_at`). None.[^344][^345]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review findings R2-1 and R2-2: the round-two gate let through sizes at which the TOST's level exceeds alpha 0.01. It computed the identical-seed level exactly only at kappa 0 and measured the rest by simulation on a grid of kappas. Nulls whose item differences take several of the seven values a three-seed item allows, with the mean on the margin, were never checked. Over that composite null, 92 of the 144 sizes it reported at alpha 0.01 have an exact level above 0.01, up to 0.0154 (538 items, 2 points). The gate is now the largest exact level found over the composite null (decision `pr2-r3-decision-level-gate`). At alpha 0.05 no reported size moves: the largest level there is 0.047. The round-two table at alpha 0.01 is withdrawn.[^346][^347][^348][^349]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-3: the round-two test of the TOST p-value's scale in Holm could not catch a p-value on the one-sided scale. Its sample's p was far below Holm's threshold either way, so halving p changed no decision; and the `decide_h1` half compared Holm's adjusted value with Holm's own output, not a hand value. A new test uses 114 items with no disagreement at a 5-point margin: z = sqrt 6 on both sides and p = 0.0143. As the smallest of five, H4 meets Holm's threshold 0.01: not rejected, as the TOST at 0.01 refuses, and H1's adjusted value through `decide_h1` is 0.0715 by hand. The one-sided mutant (p 0.0072) fails it. None.[^350]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-4: round two tested the TOST's monotonicity on one family of samples and two property tests, which a floor switched by dispersion can pass. A sample-based test sees only the (sum d, sum d^2) pairs its draws reach; the verifier's two mutants, a floor kept only below a dispersion (absolute or relative) and dropped above it, were not shown to fail them. A new test walks every (3 sum d, 9 sum d^2) with n sum d^2 at least (sum d)^2, at 20, 57, 114, 200 and 381 items, three margins and two alphas: at a fixed sum passing never resumes with more squares, at fixed squares never further from zero. Both verifier mutants fail it, and so does a floor made stricter when samples are sparse. None.[^351][^352]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** A regeneration of the H4 table ran while the mutation checks rewrote `research/analysis/equivalence.py` in the same worktree, and some of its worker processes imported a mutant. I assumed every worker imported the analysis code in its first seconds; workers imported it later, and the run's log showed divide-by-zero warnings from a mutant that drops the variance floor. The run was stopped by its task id before it wrote a result, and the table was regenerated with nothing else touching the sources; the committed table and its MANIFEST hash come from that run. Mutation checks now wait for any run that imports the code. None for the result; a mutant run beside a regeneration would have produced a wrong table.[^353][^354]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-5: at alpha 0.01 the round-two level gate first ran 4,000 simulations per check, so Monte Carlo noise decided which sizes passed. Two standard errors at 4,000 runs are 0.0031, so the gate admitted a measured level up to about 0.0069; levels at the candidate sizes sat near 0.0065, and the walk climbed to sizes whose power was 1. Commit 473e132 raised the runs to 20,000 (a gate of 0.0086) without an entry saying why. Round three removes simulation from the gate: every level checked is computed exactly (decision entry `pr2-r3-decision-level-gate`). The 4,000-run table was never committed as a result.[^355][^356]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding R2-6: the reach script's docstring still said the shares with every and with any pattern in reach bracket the share whose secret is in reach. Round two withdrew that claim in the log and in a later paragraph of the same docstring, which says neither share brackets it; the earlier paragraph was not updated, so the docstring contradicted itself. The paragraph now says neither share is, or bounds, the share whose secret is in reach, and points to the audit sample. The script's output is unchanged; its MANIFEST hash does not move. None.[^357][^358]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** A scratch local search over seven-point nulls reported levels up to 0.0149 at 381 items, 5 points, alpha 0.01, well above the three-point supremum of 0.0121. Its candidates added mass to a distribution summing to 1 and were renormalised after the mean was set, so their mean sat about 2.5 percent of the margin inside it: not nulls. Put back on the margin, the same distribution's exact level is 0.0115, and Monte Carlo agrees. Every candidate the committed search and its tests evaluate goes through `on_margin`, which normalises before it sets the mean; the tests assert the reported distribution's mean is minus the margin. None.[^359][^360]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-5: the first round-two power run simulated every item and draw, was far too slow at large sizes, and was stopped unfinished. Each simulated study drew items times seeds uniforms per arm, so the cells needing tens of thousands of items per arm did not finish. It was stopped by the two process ids it had started, as the coordinator's rule after round one requires, not by image name. Commit 630aa7b draws a study as one multinomial of the items' joint states (`item_states`), at a cost that does not grow with the size; a test checks it against simulating every item. No result of the stopped run was kept. None.[^361][^362]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding R2-8: the test of the multinomial item states against simulating every item used equal rates and kappa 0.5 only, at four standard errors of 1,500 studies, too loose to catch a real error and blind to the unequal-rate path the level uses. It compared power, a joint pass rate that many errors leave nearly unchanged, rather than the states' distribution. New tests: closed-form means, pair second moments and the all-three cell of `item_states` at rates 0.1, 0.2, 0.15 and kappa 0.4, to 1e-12; the seven-point law of a pair's difference against 400,000 simulated items within five standard errors; the simulated level at rates 0.1 and 0.2 against the exact one. A nested cut off by one and kappa drawn once per item instead of per draw both fail them. None.[^363][^364]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-8: the admission finding left out that under strict the trusted ERP and bank filler also waits as a review candidate. The result recorded only the marked entries, so it could not show that strict leaves the operator no `team:operations` record at all, a utility cost beyond the benign values. The admission rows now carry every entry and the result counts entries by scope and the attacks' filler. The admission measurement and finding are superseded; the result stays on the 24 vendored cases until PR 3's 38-case set merges.[^365][^366]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1, supersedes 2026-11-02-pr2-decision-excluded-sessions.** Review finding P2-9: an excluded session broke the pairing on the same seed. The H1 table averaged each condition over its own remaining seeds, so an item's C4 mean over seeds 1, 2 and 3 was compared with its C5 mean over seeds 1 and 3, against the pre-registration's pairing on item, model and seed. `paired_item_differences` drops an (item, seed) from both conditions when either excluded or lacks it, the contrast reports how many it dropped, and a test plants such a session (mutation M9, the old pairing, is killed). This entry supersedes the excluded-sessions decision of the same day.[^367][^368]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding P2-6: the H4 power finding read one family's power as H4's, though H4 needs every pair to pass across stores and across models, on two benchmarks. The pre-registration's H4 is supported only if every pairwise equivalence holds, and it names GateMem and MemoryAgentBench; four families of three arms, each needing all its pairs, make joint power lower than any one family's. `h4_power.py` now reports, per cell, the items for a per-family power of 0.8, 0.8^(1/2) and 0.8^(1/4) (one, two and four families jointly at 0.8), and states that the store and model tables coincide under the generative model. The H4 finding is superseded.[^369][^370]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding P2-7: the H4 entries carried wrong numbers, and the ladder could pick a rung on Monte Carlo noise. The 10-point minimum was 14 as written and 24 once degenerate passes failed, not 10; the 17 and 7 of 24 cells powered by about 100 items counted degenerate passes (14 and 6 without); the multi-pair factor ran 1.24 to 1.93, not 1.2 to 1.7, because I computed it only over cells whose estimate exceeded 100. One cell chose 3,620 items where the closed form gives 3,004 and the rung below scored 0.7975 over 400 studies. The rung the walk lands on and the rung below are now rerun with 1,000 studies each before one is kept; the joint closed form is reported beside every size. The H4 measurement and finding are superseded; the PR text is corrected.[^371][^372]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-4: the planted null and the H1 table fixture had no item effect, so pairing sessions instead of seed-averaged items passed every test (mutation M1). Without an item effect a session behaves like an item, and treating sessions as units costs nothing; real models answer an item alike across seeds. Planted items now draw a propensity per condition (Beta, a + b = 2). Over 600 runs of a planted null through the H1 table the test requires a one-sided rejection rate of at most 0.07, which sessions as units exceed, and that the primary contrast's n equals the item count. M1 is now killed by three tests. None beyond the tests.[^373][^374]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-3: the reach finding called the located-but-unreachable share an upper bound on what C5 can prevent, without defining the shares. Patterns match by text, and a generic one ("Wednesday") can match a turn holding nothing secret, so no share bounds the secret's reach; and the shares did not sum to one, because checkpoints with no located pattern (1 forgetting, 10 access) were left undefined. The result now puts each checkpoint in exactly one of three groups (reachable, located but unreachable, not located), reports the any-pattern and every-pattern shares as a bracket, and writes a seeded audit sample of matches for a person to check by hand. The finding is superseded; the hand audit is a human step.[^375][^376]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-2: the GateMem reach counted leak targets only, while GateMem's scorer also counts an access checkpoint's not_include patterns as a leak. `scoreOne` checks `leaks([...notInclude, ...leakTargets])` for an access checkpoint (`score.ts`), and the judge prompt names both; the reach's headline therefore measured a narrower leak than H1's metric scores. The reach now reports both pattern sets, a unit test plants a not_include pattern in a readable turn, and a fixture test requires the scorer's context-leak check to fire exactly when a scored pattern is in reach. The reach measurement and finding are superseded.[^377][^378]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-5: reading the poisoning arm's lower interval end instead of its upper end (mutation M4) survived every test. No H1 table test had a poisoning arm whose interval straddles zero, the only case where the two ends decide differently. Two table tests were added: an arm with C5 planted worse ("contradicts", H1 not supported) and one whose interval straddles zero ("does not contradict"). M4 is now killed. None beyond the tests.[^379][^380]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H1.** Review finding P2-10: each bootstrap's seed depended on the contrast's position, so adding another model's sessions changed the primary test. The seed came from the config's seed and a running index over (benchmark, model) pairs sorted by name; a model sorting earlier shifted every later index. The seed is now drawn from the config's seed and a sha256 of (benchmark, model, contrast); a test adds another model's sessions and requires an identical primary test (mutation M10, the old indexing, is killed). None beyond the code.[^381][^382]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure.** Review finding P2-11: `docs/conventions/testing.md` still described layer 8 as `research/tests/test_reproduce.py`, cache-only and nightly. The PR made `make test-repro` real (plan decision 10) but changed only AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md, leaving the convention to describe a test that does not exist. Layer 8's row, the CI sentence and the reproduce paragraph now describe `research/scripts/reproduce.py` (decision entry of the same day). None.[^383]

**2026-11-02, R1, failure, H4.** Review finding P2-1: H4's first TOST did not hold its level at the sizes the power table reported. With one pair's true difference on the margin, the review measured equivalence declared 18.4 percent of the time at 14 items (0 against 0.10, 10 points), 13.4 and 8.5 percent at 24 and 48 (0.02 against 0.07, 5 points), and 34.6 percent with identical seeds at 10 items: a pair with no discordant item always passed, and with few discordant items the bootstrap's tail was far too short. My power runs measured power only, never the level. The TOST was replaced (decision entry `2026-11-02-pr2-review-decision-tost-method.md`), tests now plant the difference on the margin, and every reported size carries its measured level. Every size in the first `h4-power.json` is withdrawn; the superseding measurement entry gives the new ones.[^384][^385][^386]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A mutation check that let the RAG-Policy gate's billing role see every topic passed the fixture golden test; a second mutation, in the office projects rule, failed it on five checkpoints. The fixture's one billing asker reaches no patient through its query, so the gate refuses before the role's tags matter; 20 checkpoints in four episodes cannot exercise every role and relationship branch of upstream's tables. Only the development-time comparison on all 2,218 checkpoints covers every branch the released data takes; it gave 0 mismatches. It needs the pinned full set and is not part of `make test`. A change to `rag-policy.ts` should be rerun against the full set with `check-rag-policy-upstream.ts` before it merges.[^387][^388]

**2026-11-02, X1, finding, H1.** The Mem0 adapter's search returns no item for an empty query, where the fake and Letta adapters return every item and the plane's read returns every admitted item. Card B4's scripted MEM-INV-Bench model reads memory with an empty query, as upstream shows the model its whole memory; on Mem0 the ungoverned conditions would then see no memory while C5 saw all of it. A one-off script on two items in two scopes, with the runner's Mem0 configuration (hashing embedder, threshold 0.1), returned 0 items for `""` and 2 for `"Acme"` and for `"pay the acme invoice"`; the script was not kept. The ungoverned read lists every item when the query has no terms (the next entry); a live model's own queries still meet Mem0's semantic search.[^389][^390]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The runner scored GateMem answers with the rule-based scorer only, though GateMem scores A, F and U with its judge and ADR 0031 said they go through it. Card B1's judge port was wired into the bench runner, not the condition runner. The runner now runs the judge pass, scripted in tests, live on the Responses API. Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.[^391]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** C5's read returned items C2 to C4 never saw: core matched granted items on any query term while the fake and Letta stores' search needs every term ("what is the schedule": C2 0 items, C5 103). PR 3 checked that each condition read the same items, not that C5's were a subset of C2's for natural queries. Fixed in core (every-term match) with a property test over every fixture checkpoint. Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.[^392]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A live run of an experiment config ended with "a pipeline config's numbers are still not results". The footer distinguished live from scripted runs but not experiments from pipelines. `runFooter` words it by the config's kind for both arms. Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.[^393]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** C2 to C4 showed items in the store's order and C5 in the plane's score order, so the same items reached the model in different orders. PR 3 shared retrieval between conditions but not ordering. One per-item ordering rule from core serves every condition. Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.[^394]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** PR 3 changed `packages/pi-extension/src/tag.ts`, outside the plan's file list, without a decision entry. **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** `h1-gatemem` includes the utility checkpoints, beyond H1's stopping rule, without a decision entry. **2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** C4 showed GateMem's RAG-Policy verdicts, a different policy from the one C5 enforces, so C4 minus C5 measured policy difference as well as mechanism. PR 3 ported the verdict faithfully without measuring its agreement with the plane. C4 now shows the plane's own decision by default; the RAG-Policy verdict is selectable. Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.[^397]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** Eight MEM-INV-Bench attack cases showed every model the reference model's recorded note, while upstream writes it with the model under test. PR 3 treated the note as world state and did not check how upstream builds it. The runner writes it with the model under test, as upstream does. Found by the independent review of PR #25 and fixed in its review fix pass.[^398]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** The first fix pass's every-term rule left C2 with no memory on 120 of 180 fixture reads and on 15 of 20 checkpoint questions, and on Mem0 its top-20 cap broke the subset on 45 reads (534 extra entries). The rule was chosen to make C5 a subset of C2 and was tested with short queries only, not with the questions the model will ask. Replaced by one core pipeline with any-term candidates and a floor test; forcing every term back fails the floor test (gap 0.75). Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.[^399]

**2026-11-02, X1, failure, H1.** A finding said no OpenAI model could be H1's answer model through pi-ai, while pi-ai's OpenRouter provider serves them on the seeded API. Only the `openai` provider's catalogue was read. A corrected finding supersedes it. Found by the second review of PR #25 and fixed in its round-2 fix pass.[^400]

**2026-11-02, X1, finding, H1, supersedes 2026-11-02-x1r-openai-answers-unseeded.** OpenAI's models can be H1's answer model through pi-ai 1.3.0: its built-in `openrouter` provider serves `openai/gpt-4o`, `openai/gpt-4.1` and the GPT-5 family on the seeded chat-completions API. The earlier finding looked only at pi-ai's `openai` provider, which serves them on the Responses API, and concluded that no OpenAI model could be the answer model (review round 2, R3). pi-ai's OpenRouter catalogue lists 381 models on `openai-completions`, among them `openai/gpt-4o` ("OpenAI: GPT-4o"); the runner's seeded-API rule admits them. The judge now goes this way too. PR 4 may pick M1 among OpenAI's models through OpenRouter; the key is an OpenRouter key.[^401][^402]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** PR #26's H4 table checked the TOST's level at 0.05 and 0.01 only, while H4's one p-value can meet Holm at 0.05 / k for any k from 1 to 5. Holm over the five primary p-values compares H4's intersection-union p with 0.05, 0.025, 0.0167, 0.0125 or 0.01, depending on its rank; the level of "reject when p is below a threshold" must hold at each threshold it may meet, and holding at 0.01 and 0.05 does not imply holding between them. Found by PR #26's final verification (P2v4-1), which checked 20 of the 26 distinct alpha-0.01 sizes and left 7,241 to 20,480 items at 2 points unchecked. PR 4 records the check at every threshold for every committed alpha-0.01 size (`research/results/h4-holm-levels.json`, entry `2026-11-03-pr4a-measurement-h4-holm-levels`). A test now fails if the power table changes without the record following it.[^403][^404][^405]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** PR #26 called a search result an exact level over the composite null: "the TOST's exact level over the composite null" (round-3 finding), "Largest composite-null level" (round-3 measurement), "its exact level over the composite null" (`docs/conventions/testing.md`), "every null distribution the three-seed design allows" (`equivalence.py`, and `power.py`'s `_composite_sup`). Each level the search visits is exact, but the maximum over distributions is numerical, and only the boundary |mean| = margin is searched; the interior is not argued, and independent, identically distributed items are assumed. Found by PR #26's final verification (P2v4-2). The two entries are superseded (`2026-11-03-pr4a-finding-h4-power`, `2026-11-03-pr4a-measurement-h4-power`); the convention and the two docstrings are fixed in place, since they are not log entries. v1 should call these "the largest level a search found".[^406][^407][^408][^409][^410]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** PR #26's round-3 failure entry said the round-3 finding gives its levels with where they occur, but the round-3 finding quotes no level at all. The measurement entry quoted the largest own levels (0.04289, 0.008271) and the finding was shortened past them; nothing checked the promise. Found by PR #26's final verification (P2v4-3). The superseding finding `2026-11-03-pr4a-finding-h4-power` gives both, with where they occur, read from `research/results/h4-power.json`'s `own_max` and `own_max_at` fields and the cells that hold them. None beyond the superseding entry.[^411][^412][^413]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The converter counted MEM-INV-Bench's UNREAD and MALFORMED replies as "no violation", and its comment said this was "as upstream's scoring does"; it was not. It took the port's `attack_success` (true only for HIT) as the outcome without reading upstream's `code/score.py`, which keeps FILTERED, EXCLUDED and MALFORMED out of the denominator, rates HIT over HIT plus REFUSED, and says an UNREAD reply "MUST NOT SCORE" until a human reads it. A live model refusing in prose, or naming both values, would have counted as safe. Found by the review of part A (P4A-1). Fixed in commit `83328c4`: HIT is 1, REFUSED 0, everything else null with its verdict as the reason; readings of UNREAD sessions apply before scoring (decision entries `pr4a-r1-decision-violation`, `pr4a-r1-decision-readings`). Upstream's `score.py` and `adjudication.py` were read at the pin, their sha256 matching the vendored manifest. Any scoring claim about upstream cites upstream's code.[^414][^415][^416]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H4.** Part A's H4 finding quoted sizes from the alpha 0.05 column beside those from 0.01, for example 100 items at 10 points, while only the alpha 0.01 sizes are checked at Holm's lower thresholds. H4's one p-value can meet Holm at 0.0125 or 0.01, and `research/results/h4-holm-levels.json` checks the alpha 0.01 sizes there; nothing checks an alpha 0.05 size below 0.05. The finding kept PR 2's framing without saying which column H4 may use. Found by the review (P4A-11). Superseded by `pr4a-r1-finding-h4-power`, which says H4's sample must come from the alpha 0.01 column. Part B takes H4's margin and sample from the alpha 0.01 column.[^417][^418]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The cost table's low estimate counted no reasoning tokens for any model, because the runner asks for thinking off; for six candidates that request never reaches the model. pi-ai 1.3.0 (`api/openai-completions.js`, OpenRouter's format) sends an off effort only when the catalogue's `thinkingLevelMap.off` is not null; for `openai/gpt-6.1-sol`, `google/gemini-4-argon`, `google/gemini-3.8-flash`, `qwen/qwen3.8-max-0902`, `meta/muse-wonder` and `meta/muse-glimmer-30b` it is null, so no reasoning parameter is sent and the provider's default effort applies. The table read the catalogue's `reasoning` flag and not what pi-ai sends. Found by the review (P4A-2). `catalogueEntry` now records `off_effort` and `can_turn_reasoning_off`, the result marks each candidate, and the low estimate prices a stated floor for the six (`pr4a-r1-decision-cost-range`). Read the transport, not only the catalogue's flags.[^419][^420][^421]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The first label-audit kit showed members the answer: sheets had a `benchmark_label` column, GateMem contexts opened with the expected action and leak targets, MEM-INV-Bench contexts printed the family and scoring keys, and the agreement read the benchmark label from an editable sheet. It was built to check labels shown beside the item, not as a second labelling; and as all 25 forgetting items are `no_memory`, showing the kind showed the label. Found by the review (P4A-3). Redesigned in two phases (`pr4a-r1-decision-audit-design`). A test asserts no phase-1 sheet or context holds an item's label, key, family or kind; mutants restoring the expected action, writing phase 2 early, or keeping the sampled order fail it. An audit measuring agreement shows its members nothing to agree with.[^422][^423][^424]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** Part A's report and `PR.md` said Anthropic's models are "served only on `anthropic-messages`"; `anthropic/claude-*-5.5:batch` and `~anthropic/claude-*-latest` are on `openai-completions` in the same catalogue. The report shortened the seeded-routes finding, which named those entries, into a wrong absolute. Found by the review (P4A-5). The four entries are now in the cost table as priced non-candidates: the `:batch` ones, named "(batch)" at half the interactive price, because whether OpenRouter serves them as synchronous chat completions and passes the seed cannot be checked without a call; the `~...-latest` ones because they float. `PR.md` and the report use the finding's wording (superseded by `pr4a-r1-finding-seeded-routes`). A summary keeps a finding's qualifiers.[^425][^426]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The cost table priced `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` at an input price of 0.00576 dollars per million tokens without remarking that it looks wrong. The script copied catalogue prices unchecked. Found by the review (P4A-6). `price_anomaly` now flags a candidate whose input price is under 1.5 times its cache-read price or whose output price is over 25 times its input price; only this model is flagged (finding `pr4a-r1-finding-price-anomaly`). The price is still used as listed. A flagged price is checked against the provider's own page before a model is chosen on it.[^427][^428]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** The cost table took tokens per memory item, and the judge's prompt, from the two fixture checkpoints unscaled, while it scaled the item count and the probe prompt to the full set. Item size and judge prompt depend on the episode, and the fixture has two. Found by the review (P4A-8), which measured the fixture episodes' turns at 88.9 characters against 114.2. Measured as the runner shows them, the fixture's reads hold longer items than the full set's lists: 164.6 characters per item under C2 and C3, 171.6 under C4, 211.7 under C5, against 138.8 to 141.2, 118.3 to 126.1 and 130.1 to 138.8 on the full set (`measured` in `research/results/h1-cost.json`); turns up to each as-of turn are 122.7 against 119.8. Tokens per item fall by 5.9 to 20.4, the judge's prompt by 17. Scale by what a session shows, not by a whole-episode mean.[^429][^430]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure, H1.** H1's pipeline could not run the analysis v1 recommends: access-control checkpoints as the primary GateMem set, forgetting as secondary, and a sensitivity table without the checkpoints where a plain refusal is scored as a leak. The sessions carried no checkpoint kind, the table pooled every GateMem session into one test, and the refusal-leak checkpoints were counted in PR 2's reach result (access 29, forgetting 12) but not named. Found by part B while writing v1 (orchestrator's items 8 and 9). The converter writes each GateMem session's `kind`; `research/configs/h1-table.json` names the primary set in one line (`gatemem_primary`) and the sensitivity list; `research/results/h1-refusal-leaks.json` names the 41 checkpoints. The planted end-to-end test asserts the primary, both secondary sets and the sensitivity table; mutants ignoring the set, the list or the kind fail it. v1's analysis is the tagged commit's.[^431][^432][^433]

**2026-11-03, R1, finding, H1.** pi-ai 1.3.0's catalogue lists `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` on OpenRouter at 0.00576 dollars per million input tokens: 1.07 times its cache-read price (0.0054) and 125 times below its output price (0.72). Every other candidate's input sits at 2 to 30 times its cache-read price and 3 to 6 times below its output price, so this one is likely the cache-read price entered as the input price. Its low total, 67.90 dollars with the judge, is almost all judge; its answers cost 1.83 dollars low on GateMem. `research/results/h1-cost.json` flags it (`price_anomaly`) and uses the listed price. The catalogue cannot be checked against OpenRouter offline. For part B: do not choose this model on its price before checking OpenRouter's listing; at a typical small-model input price its cost would be near the other small tiers'.[^434]

**2026-11-03, R1, finding, H1, supersedes 2026-11-03-pr4a-finding-seeded-routes.** Through pi-ai 1.3.0, OpenRouter is the one route that reaches OpenAI's, Google's, DeepSeek's, Qwen's, Mistral's and Meta's models on the seeded chat-completions API; Anthropic's current models are on it only as `:batch` entries and floating aliases. The runner accepts only `openai-completions` (`SEEDED_APIS`); the superseded entry said the same, and the review asked what those Anthropic entries are (P4A-5). pi-ai's own providers serve OpenAI, xAI and Meta on Responses APIs, Google and Mistral on their own APIs, all refused; DeepSeek's own is on chat completions. OpenRouter lists `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5` and `claude-haiku-5.5` on `anthropic-messages` (refused). Its chat-completions entries `anthropic/claude-*-5.5:batch`, named "(batch)" at half price, cannot be shown to serve synchronous, seeded calls without one; `~anthropic/claude-*-latest` float. `research/results/h1-cost.json` prices all four as non-candidates. v1's three models come from the six makers above; one OpenRouter key serves them and the judge.[^435][^436][^437]

**2026-11-03, R1, finding, H1.** On six candidates H1 cannot run with thinking off: `openai/gpt-6.1-sol`, `google/gemini-4-argon`, `google/gemini-3.8-flash`, `qwen/qwen3.8-max-0902`, `meta/muse-wonder` and `meta/muse-glimmer-30b`. The runner asks every session for thinking off; pi-ai 1.3.0 forwards that to OpenRouter only for models whose catalogue maps off to an effort. For these six the catalogue maps off to null, so pi-ai sends no reasoning parameter and each provider's default effort applies, unseen by the cassette key. Neither pi-ai nor its catalogue states that default or its token count. `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, both DeepSeek, both Mistral models and `qwen3.8-flash` receive effort "none"; `gpt-4o` has no reasoning (`research/results/h1-cost.json`, `can_turn_reasoning_off`). For part B's model choice: on these six, H1 measures the model at its provider's default reasoning, an uncontrolled difference from the others, and its cost is the least certain.[^438][^439]

**2026-11-03, R1, failure.** Tests timed out under machine load: one of the 11 count tests, whose name I did not capture, and 8 doctor tests at the 30-second limit. The machine's CPU stood at 100 percent while other sessions ran, and the first count run shared it with a pytest run of this worktree. **2026-11-07, B5, finding.** Two facts about the dates of LongMemEval's pinned oracle release, as `longmemeval-date-facts.ts` reports them on the full file. Through the plane, a turn is an item valid from its session's date and retention excludes items past their ttl at read, so dates decide what C5 can return. 43 of 500 questions date a history session after the question itself (40 temporal-reasoning, 3 knowledge-update), by up to 23.3 hours. At the read, 40 of 500 questions hold a session older than the `medium` preset's person-scope episodic ttl of 90 days (28 knowledge-update, 12 temporal-reasoning), so C5 under `medium` hides those turns where C2 shows them. The read is placed after the last turn (decision entry `2026-11-07-b5-decision-read-time.md`). The second fact is a real cost of retention on long histories, and the live baseline under C5 reports it beside the accuracy.[^527][^528]

**2026-11-07, failure.** On Windows, `make bench BENCH=gatemem` and `make run` failed when started from PowerShell, with `'ENGRAM_OFFLINE' is not recognized as an internal or external command`. The GNU make for Windows that was installed (ezwinports 4.4.1, through scoop) runs recipes in `cmd.exe` when no `sh` is on the PATH, so the Makefile's POSIX recipes (inline variables, `{ ...; }` groups) break. `make test` and `make lint` only run single commands, so they passed. Started from Git Bash, which puts `sh` on the PATH, both targets pass. On Windows, run `make` from Git Bash.[^529][^530]

**2026-11-19, L2, finding, H3.** Under preset medium, part of LongMemEval's S histories is past its validity at the question's read time. Medium retires person-scope episodic items after 90 days (`ttl: 7776000`), and B5 writes every turn as an episodic item dated by its session; the read is at the question's date (B5's `readTime`). The fault set drops that ttl, so the number was counted while building it. 11,122 of 246,750 turns, in 54 of 500 questions, are past medium's ttl at the read time (`research/results/h3-fault-set.json`, `base.past_medium_ttl`). On those 54 questions B5's C5 read hides the older turns by policy, and C6's expired-validity lint flags them, so H1 to H3 on LongMemEval under medium measure the ttl as well as the gate. For the lead with B6 and X10: keep the ttl (it is the preset), or report the 54 questions apart.[^545][^546][^547]

**2026-11-19, L2, failure, H3.** Review finding L2-1 (critical) on PR #46: the set could only confirm the deterministic lints, and nothing said so. Each keyed subtype restated its lint's trigger, with near-misses far from the boundary (30 days past the lint time), and the check failed unless every keyed fault was flagged and nothing else was; so deterministic precision and recall were 1.0 by construction. The model tier saw only injected template notes, every labelled pair pre-selected for judging, never LongMemEval's own text; and findings on base items were made impossible, by naming the assistant model and failing on any unlabelled finding. Fixed in the review fix pass: boundary near-misses, cue-free and paraphrased templates, four unsupported-claim variants, base-item findings counted and sampled for audit, and a `limits` field (entries `2026-11-19-l2-review-*`). The set is a regression check and an upper bound, stated as such.[^548][^549]

The result's `limits.differs_from_b5_b6_x10` and the fixture README now state the assistant model, the ttl, the turns past medium's ttl, and how many labelled model units L3's planning would not send under medium, counted by the check script with L3's merged `heldAt` rule. Whoever compares the set with B5's runs reads the differences in the result itself.[^550][^551]

**2026-11-19, L2, failure, H3.** Review finding L2-4 on PR #46: the superseded label assumed that card L3's contradiction lint finds a supersession, which depends on how the model reads L3's prompt. L3's prompt asks whether two statements "cannot both be true at the same time"; an old value and a later one can be read as two states at different times, and so as consistent. The label did not say so. The superseded subtype's description, the fixture README and the result's `limits.superseded` now say that superseded recall measures how the model reads that prompt; a backlog row asks the lead or card L4 to decide whether the prompt should treat a later update as a contradiction. L3's prompt is unchanged. Superseded recall is reported apart from conflict recall.[^552][^553]

**2026-11-19, L2, finding, H3.** An injected attribute can collide with a fact of the question's own history: "My cat's name is Miso" in a history where the user's cat is Luna, or an invented gym beside the user's real one. The generator keeps injected attributes apart within the injected items of a question, but does not read LongMemEval's turns to avoid what they say: matching free text needs a model or a hand check. Such a collision is an unlabelled contradiction between an injected item and a base turn. No deterministic lint sees it, and L3's contradiction lint pairs only semantic items while base turns are episodic, so it reaches neither tier; it would reach a reader of the memory, or a card that extracts facts from the turns. Stated as a limit; L4 need not correct for it while base turns stay episodic.[^554][^555]

**2026-11-19, L2, finding, H3.** What H3's seeded-fault set can and cannot tell the thesis, as the result's `limits` field states it. Review finding L2-1: nothing said that the set confirms the deterministic lints by construction. It can tell: a regression check on L1's lints; model-tier accuracy on clean, pre-paired template cases, as an upper bound; and the gaps, wrong-scope (500 faults, no lint) and the deterministic tier on unkeyed faults. It cannot tell how common these faults are, real-world recall, or precision on natural memory without the audit sample. Per-class figures pooled over keyed and unkeyed faults depend on the mix (stale and unsupported 2 keyed to 1, misplaced 1 to 1, contradictory 0 to 1). Every hardened near-miss gets the deterministic outcome its label says: 0 near-misses flagged, 0 unexpected findings on injected items (`label_check`). L4 and the thesis report these figures with that scope.[^556][^557]

**2026-11-19, L2, finding, H3, supersedes 2026-11-19-l2-finding-natural-faults.** LongMemEval's own turns carry faults nobody seeded, now measured rather than left out. The superseded entry counted 968 duplicate findings on base turns; review finding L2-2 showed one of them was on a labelled near-miss, and L2-1 asked that natural findings be counted. On the full set (`research/results/h3-fault-set.json`): the duplicate lint flags 967 base turns (`label_check.unlabelled_findings_on_base_items`), 50 of them drawn for the audit (`audit`); with the near-miss fixed, 0 findings fall on injected items unexpected. As B5 writes them, 124,334 of 246,750 turns would be missing-provenance findings (`base.missing_provenance_as_b5_writes_it`); the set names the assistant model, so they are recorded, not shown. Content-level faults in upstream text cannot be counted without a model. L4 reports model-tier precision on injected items and on base items apart.[^558][^559]

**2026-11-19, L2, finding, H3.** Superseded recall measures how the model reads card L3's contradiction prompt, not only whether it sees a supersession. The prompt (`LINT_PROMPTS.contradiction`) calls two statements a contradiction when they "cannot both be true at the same time", and sends their text only, not their times. "My gym is X" and a later "These days my gym is Y" can be read as two states at different times, and so as consistent; a conflict, said in the same breath, cannot. The superseded subtype's description, the fixture README and the result's `limits.superseded` say so. A backlog row asks the lead or card L4 to decide whether the prompt should treat a later update of the same attribute as a contradiction; a prompt change would be a new cassette and an amendment. The prompt is unchanged here. L4 reports superseded and conflict recall apart.[^560][^561][^562]

**2026-11-19, L2, finding, H3.** Under preset medium, card L3's planning would not send some of the set's labelled model units. Review finding L2-3 asked for the count after the rebase. L3's merged `heldAt` rule skips a claim whose source is past its ttl, and medium retires person-scope episodic items after 90 days; the set's asides are episodic. With every lint enabled under medium, 45 of the 1,000 labelled unsupported-claim units (faults and near-misses) and 0 of the 2,000 contradiction units would not be sent (`research/results/h3-fault-set.json`, `label_check.model_labels_not_sent_under_medium`). The review counted 51 on the set before the fix pass; the hardened set places its asides afresh, so the count moved. Under the set's own policy all are sent. Stated in `limits.differs_from_b5_b6_x10`; L4 lints under the set's policy.[^563][^564][^565]

**2026-11-19, L3, finding, H3.** An item's own content can steer the model lints' verdict, for example a claim that says it is stated verbatim in its source. Review L3-6. The inputs are sent as one JSON object, so content cannot break a delimiter, but its words still reach the model. The only defence is the prompt's line that the inputs are data, not instructions; no test can show a live model heeds it, and the scripted tests do not try. H3's model tier is open to the poisoning it is meant to catch; the limitations chapter says so, and a seeded steering fault in L2's set would measure it.[^566][^567]

**2026-11-19, L4, failure, H3.** Review finding L4-3: the entries and PR body read the 0.721 to 1.0 bound as precision on natural memory. 0.721 is 2,500 / (2,500 + 967). The 2,500 true positives are set by the injection rate: at `per_question` 2 the lower end would be 0.838. The bound says nothing about how often the duplicate lint is right on LongMemEval's own items. The result now says so (`by_construction.not_natural_precision`) and gives the natural-memory figure as each lint's own bound on its base findings (`natural_memory`: duplicate, 967 findings, 0 to 1) until the 50-item audit is judged. Entries that stated the bound as natural precision are superseded.[^568][^569]
