Superseded by `2026-11-19-l4-review-finding-live-count`, which states both and gives the reviewer's cost table. The run's size is the lead's decision, through an amendment.[^573][^574]

**2026-11-19, L4, failure, H3.** Review finding L4-6: the PR body and `finding-by-construction` said no deterministic lint reads text, which is wrong: the duplicate lint compares content hashes. A shortening of the result's own sentence. Both now use the result's wording: no deterministic lint is built for what makes an unkeyed item faulty, which is in its text only. None beyond the wording.[^575]

**2026-11-19, L4, finding, H3, supersedes 2026-11-19-l4-finding-by-construction.** Which floor numbers hold by construction, as the result's `by_construction` states beside L2's `limits`. L2's review (finding L2-1) showed the keyed subtypes restate their lints' triggers; this card's review (L4-1, L4-3, L4-6) corrected the rest. By construction: keyed recall and precision of 1.0, overall and per keyed lint; unkeyed recall 0, as no deterministic lint is built for what makes an unkeyed item faulty, which is in its text only; wrong-scope recall 0 under either tier, as its labels name no lint. Measured: the 967 base-item findings, whose precision is bounded only by 0 and 1; the pooled 0.721 to 1.0 moves with the injection rate and is not precision on natural memory. The floor is a regression check on L1 until the lead decides H3's deterministic claim.[^576][^577]

**2026-11-19, L4, finding, H3.** The generator's injected sentences open with recognisable phrases ("For the record, my ...", "As it stands, my ...", "These days my ...") that LongMemEval's own turns lack, so a model can tell an injected item from a base one. Found by the review of PR #49. The pools are cue-free between a fault and its near-miss (L2's restatement-pool decision), not between injected and natural text. Under L3's planning every unit judges an injected item: contradiction pairs only semantic items, which base turns are not, but an unsupported-claim unit's source can be a base turn. A model, or a reader, seeing both could tell them apart by the opener. L2's generator is not changed here. A backlog row for card L2.[^578][^579]

**2026-11-19, RV1, finding, H1.** A deterministic failure of the answer model, such as a 400 for a C1 prompt over the model's context limit, stops a live run for good: nothing records it, the outer retry does not retry it, and no exclusion reason exists for it. Found by the review of PR #55, outside its findings. The answer path records only replies, and a 400 is not one; every resume makes the same call and fails the same way. Not fixed here: a recorded answer failure with an exclusion reason (for example `answer refused by the provider`) is a protocol choice for the lead, like the turn limit. Backlog row "Deterministic answer failure". Before the tag, the lead decides how such a session is excluded, or the configs are checked against each model's context limit.[^580][^581]

**2026-11-19, RV1, finding.** Two processes writing the same `--out` folder are not guarded: both append to results.jsonl and usage.jsonl, and the rows of the two interleave. Found by the review of PR #55, outside its findings. The journal opens its files in append mode and takes no lock. Not fixed here. A lock file in the output folder, taken when the journal opens and refused while another process holds it, would guard it; on Windows its holder's death must be detected. Backlog row "Concurrent writers". Until then, one run per output folder is the operator's rule.[^582]

**2026-11-20, RV8, finding.** OpenRouter's public model list, read on 2026-11-20 after card RV8 priced it, gives `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-0813` at $1.32 and $3.96 per million input and output tokens. RV8's price file, from the same day, has $0.22 and $9.50. It is not known whether the price changed during the day or the price file is wrong. Either way, the cost of the lead's chosen models (M1 DeepSeek v4 pro) cannot be read off RV8's table. GLM 5.3 Flash, the new M2, is missing from RV8's price file. RV8 fetches the prices again, adds `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, and prices the lead's set before any `spend_ceiling_usd` is written into a config. It also states which fetch its numbers come from.[^583]

**2026-11-20, RV6, finding.** The B1 reference run goes through the runner's C1, which sends no temperature (pi sets only thinking off), while card B1's port of the reference agent runs at temperature 0.2 and 4,096 tokens. The B1 harness (`packages/bench/scripts/run-gatemem.ts`) wires only scripted models; the runner's C1 is GateMem's long-context prompt (ADR 0031, decision 3) and the only live path, so B1 uses it. The prompt, the memory block (the last 300 turns), the judge and its scoring are B1's port; the temperature is the provider's default (`packages/runner/src/conditions.ts`). The proposed tolerance's 3-point margin covers this in part, not by measurement. Backlog row: a temperature for C1 (a cache key change for C1) or a live path for the B1 harness, if the lead wants the reference run at upstream's sampling.[^584][^585][^586]

**2026-11-20, RV8, finding, supersedes 2026-11-20-rv8-finding-deepseek-price.** OpenRouter's list moved during 2026-11-20: deepseek-v4-pro-0813 went from 0.22 in and 9.5 out to 1.32 and 3.96, deepseek-v4.1-flash from 0.051 and 0.567 to 0.3 and 1.2, and the model count from 479 to 477. The orchestrator's finding of the same day saw the new DeepSeek price against the file; the price file was right for its fetch, the list had changed. A second fetch (sha256 f494e908...a3d0, 926,144 bytes), repeated once with identical bytes, replaced every price; only those two models differ. z-ai/glm-5.3-flash is listed at 0.0399 in, 0.0784 out. Prices are list prices of a moment; recheck on the day each run starts. The file keeps both fetches' hashes.[^587]

**2026-11-21, finding, H4.** pi-ai 1.3.0's OpenRouter catalogue lists `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, the lead's M2, with `thinkingLevelMap.off` null, so the runner cannot turn its reasoning off. v1's Models section excludes any model for which pi-ai sends no reasoning parameter, since it runs at the provider's unstated default effort. M1 (`deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-0813`) and M3 (`mistralai/mistral-large-4-0`) map off to `none`, so both honour it. Card RV8's decision entry said the catalogue does not list GLM 5.3 Flash; it does, and its conclusion, an off switch that is ignored, stands. The cost table already prices M2 that way. v1 states the conflict, and the lead decides before M2's first run, after Gate C: keep M2 at its default effort and say so, or another M2 from a third maker that can turn reasoning off.[^588][^589][^590]

**2026-11-21, B10, failure.** The first pass of card B10 shipped two gaps the review found: the endpoint check accepted any loopback port when called without the proxy, and the decision entry's claim that ENGRAM_OFFLINE is read on every call had no test. The first pass tested the endpoint check only against the running proxy, and tested the offline refusal only at start. A mutation reading the offline flag only at start passed all 17 tests of the first pass. Both are fixed and tested in the fix pass, and the mutation now fails the new test. Every claim in a decision entry gets a test that fails when the claim is broken.[^591][^592]

**2026-11-21, B11, finding, H5, supersedes 2026-11-21-b11-finding-serve-retrieval.** Through `engram serve`, C5's read was not only ranked differently from C2's: its candidates differed. The plane's default read searches the store, and the files store's search needs every query term, while core's `retrieve`, which C2 uses, takes any term. The earlier finding said only the ranking differed, and that was wrong. Review B11-1. The pre-registration's "Shared retrieval for C2 to C5" says no condition uses a store's own search. C5 now reads through a plane built with `retrieval: { pipeline: "core", limit }`, as the runner's coreReadPlane does. The plane test reads "reservation change", where each item holds only "reservation": C5 returns C2's three items in C2's order. With the core read removed, the test fails. No change to `engram serve` is needed before card X11; the launcher hosts C5's plane.[^593][^594][^595]

**2026-11-21, B11, finding, H5.** Under `engram serve`, C5 judged validity and retention by the wall clock (2026), while every item was dated at memory_time (2024-05-15). Medium's person episodic rule has a 90-day ttl, so the plane allowed an episodic write and then filtered it as not active on the next read. Review B11-2. Rows would have depended on when the run happened, and C5 would have hidden what C2 shows for a reason no policy states. The plane's clock is now memory_time. The script's task 19 writes an episodic note, and C5's read on task 16 returns it at rank 2 of 3 (`filtered="0"`). The plane test reads an episodic item back at its own date. The fixture cassettes were re-recorded by `make fixtures`'s tau2 lines, from the scripted agents.[^596][^597][^598]

**2026-11-21, R2, finding.** Several benchmarks with a permissive top-level licence inherit restricted terms, or state their terms inconsistently. The intake note lists only licences checked at the source, and the top-level licence is not the whole answer. AgentMemBench is unreleased and built on LoCoMo (CC-BY-NC-4.0). StreamMemBench is MIT, but it derives from EgoLife, whose repository licence allows only non-commercial redistribution. PersonaMem is MIT, but it is generated from PersonaHub (CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0). MemDerive's evaluator carries a CC-BY-NC judge prompt. deepset's card states both apache-2.0 and cc-by-4.0, which are both admissible. lethe gives its adversarial ForgetEval set as 385 cases in its README and 112 in its own doc. Any card that pins one of these resolves the term first. lethe's count is read at the pin.[^599][^600][^601][^602][^603]

**2026-11-21, R3, failure.** Three tables could not be read reliably, and part of HEAMS's appendix was skimmed rather than read. arXiv's HTML page for SAFE-Mem held no paper text, so both papers were read as text extracted from their PDFs (`pdftotext -layout`), which lost the column alignment of HEAMS Tables 4 and 6 and SAFE-Mem Table 2. HEAMS has 69 pages; the tables of Appendices C.1 and C.3 and the prompts of D.1 and D.3 were skimmed. No number in `docs/research/positioning.md` or this card's log entries comes from those three tables unless the paper's prose states it too; SAFE-Mem's EHRAgent results are taken from its Sec. 5.3 text. Nothing in the skimmed parts is cited. A reader who needs a number from those tables reads the PDF itself.[^604][^605][^606]

**2026-11-21, R4, failure.** EUR-Lex's HTML pages for both acts could not be read by a script: curl got HTTP 202 with an empty body, and the web fetch tool saw an empty page. EUR-Lex answers automated clients with a browser challenge. The texts were fetched instead from the Publications Office's CELEX resource (`http://publications.europa.eu/resource/celex/32024R1689` and `32016R0679`, asking for XHTML), which serves the same Official Journal documents EUR-Lex shows. The script's own `--fetch` gets byte-identical files (the config's sha256 matched). The config cites the Official Journal reference, the ELI and the Publications Office URL for each act; a reader can open the same text on EUR-Lex by its ELI.[^607][^608]

**2026-11-21, R4, failure.** The first parser counted tests that never run as evidence, and could not read most `.each` titles. It matched call text anywhere, in comments and strings included. It read nesting from indentation and checked only the describes a row named, so a skipped describe it did not name, `.skipIf`, an options `skip`, and `if (false)` went unseen. It read `.each` only over string lists with one `%s`, missing `] as const)`, `%i`, `$key` rows and `.for`. The independent review's probes showed each case. The bracket scanner of the fix pass refuses all 19 probes in `research/tests/test_crosswalk.py`, and mutants removing comment skipping, single-quoted strings, describe skips, enclosure, options and conditions each fail a test. No committed row named a test the old parser wrongly accepted: all 97 earlier tests pass the new rules, six of them once marked enclosed.[^609][^610]

**2026-11-21, X14, finding.** AMSB's leak-aws-key survives every setting, card E2.9's guards at block included. Its canary is `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE`, AWS's documented example key, which the secret guard allows by design, as gitleaks does. Of AMSB's three sensitive_data attacks, the guards at block stop two (the GitHub token and the keyed connection string) and pass the example key. AMSB's grade under the guards counts that example as a critical breach; the table says so beside it.[^611][^612]

**2026-11-21, X14, finding.** Three guards and surfaces merged before this card are not exercised by it: protect_trusted, the verified actors of card E8.12, and the span export, who-can-read and framework kit of cards E8.13, E2.13 and E8.14. AMSB has no scenario that rewrites, retires or evicts another writer's item, and the surfaces sit above Plane.write and Plane.read, which is all the card drives. Turning protect_trusted on changes no row; the config's `not_run` names each. A rewrite or eviction attack family, and a surface-level suite, are later extensions.[^613]

**2026-11-22, L7, finding, H3.** `make fixtures` re-records the model cassettes of l3-lint-fixture and h3-lint-model-fixture but not the NLI scores. The next card that regenerates fixtures needs to know what keeps the tiered replays working. The NLI score cassettes are keyed on pair text only, so they survive a re-recording of the model cassettes; the band's model calls stay hits while the fault fixtures' text is unchanged. If that text changes, `make nli-scores CONFIG=` must record the new pairs, which needs the network once to fetch the 568 MB model. Nothing now. Arm P3 is proposed, not registered: this is an engineering check, not a thesis result.[^614][^615]

**2026-11-22, L7, finding, H3.** On card L4's fixture the pinned NLI model's contradiction scores do not separate contradictions from unrelated pairs. The tier's flag band is only worth having if a high score means a contradiction. Pairs the contradiction lint pairs by shared terms, such as a chess club against a dry cleaner, score as high as the injected contradictions: 79 of 113 pairs score 0.99 or more and 61 score 0.999 or more by both directions (`score_counts`). Every contradiction fault scored above 0.993, so none was dropped, but most high scores are not contradictions. On this fixture the tier is a drop filter, never a judge. A flag band would need another model, a premise and hypothesis framing, or a different threshold source. Arm P3 is proposed, not registered: this is an engineering check, not a thesis result.[^616]

**2026-11-25, finding.** Work the gates need has no card or no pull request on main: pre-tag conditions 2, 3, 5 and 7, H5's prerequisites, Gate D's request-identity check and the Knowledge dogfood. The plan checked what Gates C and D rest on before selecting. On main at `72e7ca0e`: no runner code checks a programme-wide cap (`grep -rE 'billed|programme' packages/runner/src` finds nothing); the preregistration says items 14 and 15 are not built; no config names OpenRouter endpoints; item 30's draft is owed; ADR 0035's check and item 28's card have no file. Section 9 of the plan names each; the spend cap is question 1 to the lead. The orchestrator writes the cards.[^617][^618][^619][^620]

**2026-11-25, finding, H1.** The research's plans assume schema changes with a `schema_version` bump, and after the tag any bump moves registered bytes. The master plan lists eight schema pull requests with bumps; card E2.8 found that C3 and C4 embed the parsed policy, its version included, in their prompts. Since the policy shares `SCHEMA_VERSION` with items and events, a bump after 1 December makes H1's live cassettes miss on main and moves the fault-set hashes `research/configs/h3-lint-model.json` pins. The second plan bumps nothing. A backlog row asks for an ADR separating the versions before any schema plan returns.[^621][^622][^623]

**2026-11-25, E2.21, finding, H1.** Before building ADR 0034's one-year cap and first-write rule, the pre-registration guard's survey of who writes grants on a first write. No registered result or cassette may move before `prereg-v1`. GateMem's mapping gives every unrestricted relationship without an end the grant expiry `OPEN_END`, `9999-12-31T23:59:59Z`, and every turn is a first write through `Plane.write`. A read-only count with the bench's own `mapCheckpoint`: on the fixture subset, 1,633 of 2,490 turn writes carry grants beyond a year (all 20 checkpoints); on the full pinned set (commit `a983828b`, 8 of 8 files matching), 197,143 of 325,530 (2,053 of 2,218 checkpoints). None is expired or of another class. The rule would refuse each. `h1-gatemem-reach.json` (sha256 `9607555c...`) records `denied_writes` 0 and `reachable_granted` 466, so its bytes move, as would C5's GateMem prompts and cassettes. Unregistered: `fixtures/profiles/twin-reads.json` writes an expired grant. Stopped before coding; the lead decides.[^624][^625][^626][^627][^628]

**2026-11-25, finding, H1.** The H1 runbook in section 7 of `docs/tasks/prereg-v1-plan.md` has no step for the operator's credit check, which now caps H1. Pre-tag condition 7's fallback (read OpenRouter's credit at least every two hours while any shard runs; stop every shard when the credit left falls below one session's high estimate times the running shards, plus 10 percent) is stated only in the pre-registration. The lead chose it for H1 on 2026-11-25. `grep -n -i "credit" docs/tasks/prereg-v1-plan.md` finds nothing. The runbook's live commands also still name `gpt-5.6-terra`, not M1 (backlog row "The smoke run's price and commands"). Before 1 December the runbook gains the check as a step, with the threshold worked out per arm; a documents-only change the answers card or the operator can make. No card is written for it.[^629][^630]

**2026-11-26, R6, finding, H1.** M1's pinned endpoint, Wafer's `wafer/us`, lists quantisation `mxfp4`, a 4-bit format, and the endpoint pin uses tags (`wafer/us`, `openai`) in OpenRouter's `order`. The lead should know before the smoke run what the rule's choice serves, and what was not checked offline. Of M1's thirteen seeded endpoints, the three cheapest at H1's mix are 4-bit (`wafer/us`, `wafer/mxfp4`) or of unknown quantisation (`alibaba`); the fp8 ones start at DeepInfra's `deepinfra/fp8/flex`, about 773 dollars for H1 GateMem. Whether OpenRouter's `order` matches a full endpoint tag, and whether the slug `openai` also admits `openai/flex` and `openai/fast`, was not checked: no call is made. The smoke run prints the upstream that served each call (card RV6), which settles it. If the lead wants full precision, `quantizations: ["fp8"]` with a new pin is one config PR before the run.[^631]

**2026-11-26, X20, finding, H1.** pi-ai carries an HTTP error's status at the start of `errorMessage` only for errors raised before streaming; an error OpenRouter sends mid-stream reaches it with no status. The card asks for a finding if the status is not reliably readable. The openai SDK raises a mid-stream `error` chunk as an `APIError` with an undefined status, and pi-ai's `formatProviderError` then returns its bare message, for example "Insufficient credits". Such an error matches neither the stopping set nor a refusal and stops the run as before (tested). Its status, when in a body pi-ai folded in as `"code"`, is still read. No mid-stream error is ever excluded; a mid-stream credit failure stops the run under its own message, not as a ProviderStopError.[^632][^633]

**2026-11-27, R6, finding, H1.** OpenRouter's keyless endpoint list for the four models was read once more on 2026-11-27 and compared with the committed 2026-11-26 responses. The new responses were not committed. Finding 2 needs every pinned endpoint to list every parameter sent, and the snapshot is a day old. No endpoint was added or removed, and no endpoint's `supported_parameters` or quantisation changed. The pinned endpoints are unchanged. Four M1 prices moved: Alibaba's two endpoints doubled, Baidu's rose about fivefold, and Relace's input fell. None of them is pinned, and `wafer/us` is still the cheapest seeded endpoint. Unchecked offline: pi-ai sends `max_completion_tokens` where OpenRouter lists `max_tokens`, and it also sends `store` and `stream_options`. If `require_parameters` refuses these, the first call fails and the run stops, which is the safe direction. The smoke run is the first live test of these points.[^634][^635]

**2026-11-27, R6, finding, H1.** The smoke run and B1 now have one more pass criterion, written into their runbook (`docs/tasks/prereg-v1-plan.md`, section 7, "The live checks before the tag"). Every recorded upstream must be the provider the snapshot gives the pin: `Wafer` for M1, `OpenAI` for GPT-5.4 and the judges. Review finding 3: nothing checked that the pinned endpoint served the call. The recorded upstream is a provider name, which cannot tell tags apart. `Wafer` covers both `wafer/us` and `wafer/mxfp4`. `OpenAI` covers `openai`, `openai/flex` and `openai/fast`. So it catches another provider, not another tag. Whether `order` with a full tag admits only that tag is untested. The registered pass rule in H1's Stopping rule is unchanged, since R6 does not sanction editing it. Proving B1's standard tier needs a later card that records each call's tier (backlog row "The resolved model of each call").[^636][^637][^638][^639]

**2026-11-27, X20, finding, H1.** Card X20 disagrees with itself on a plain 400. Its Goal names "a 400" as an exclusion. Its acceptance requires `rv1.test.ts` to pass unchanged, and that test expects a `400 Bad Request` to stop the run. The disagreement surfaced in the fix pass of 2026-11-27. The orchestrator settled it for X20 in favour of the acceptance, so a plain 400 stops. If a per-prompt 400 recurs on one item, an H1 run stops there at every resume. The runbook routes that case to the lead (`docs/tasks/prereg-v1-plan.md`, section 7). A card after H1's `MERGE=4`, when the runner may change again, decides whether a plain 400 becomes an exclusion. That card would also update RV1's two tests. A ready patch was drafted in the fix pass, but it is not on the branch.[^640][^641][^642]

**2026-11-27, X20, finding, H1.** The five-item streak counts within one process. A resume starts it at zero, and each shard counts its own. The final review's finding 5. The count is module state in cassette.ts, and a resume skips the finished sessions without counting them. Two consequences were not written down before. A run-wide misread can cost up to four items per shard per resume before the stop. MERGE's byte-for-byte claim holds only when no streak in the unsharded run would have crossed a shard boundary. A new test pins the reset: after a streak stop, a resume replays the fifth item's recorded refusal, counts one, and goes on to the sixth item (2 new calls). The doc comment on `REFUSAL_STREAK_LIMIT`, the runner README and the H1 runbook say so. No code change. The operator reads a streak stop per shard.[^643][^644][^645]
